# Changelog
## Unreleased
- 2026-11-27 19:55 UTC: The heal-history ring + the --doctor watchdog status line in `vfio.sh` (R99 — the accepted R98 improvement suggestions, "ok do it as last" — the last change of this arc):
  - feature 1 (the heal-history ring): every real watchdog action (hung hooks killed / daemon restart / recovered / still-dead-reboot-needed) now appends one timestamped line to `${VFIO_DYNAMIC_DIR:-/var/lib/vfio-dynamic}/autoheal-history.log`, trimmed to the LAST 20 events — the heal history survives journal rotation and journal-permission limits (a plain user often cannot read the system journal), while the journal keeps the full detail. Cooldown-wait ticks deliberately stay journal-only (they are non-actions; the ring holds heal events, so 20 lines cover a long history). Best-effort, never fatal.
  - feature 2 (--unstuck shows the story): the verbose run (what --unstuck executes) now prints the LAST 10 history events up front — the recent auto-heal story without needing journalctl access — before performing the live detect-and-heal pass (and "no auto-heal history yet — nothing has ever wedged" on a fresh install).
  - feature 3 (the --doctor status line): the health dashboard's section 3 now reports the watchdog: timer ACTIVE (every 2 minutes) / installed but timer INACTIVE / not installed, plus the age of the last heal derived from the cooldown stamp, plus the history-ring size. Read-only, non-root-safe, informational (never affects the verdict); the JSON mode is untouched.
  - regression locks: 7 new R99 asserts in regression/live-attach-regression.sh (the ring file, the 20-event trim, the restart + still-dead ring lines, the verbose history header, the doctor status line, the last-heal-age derivation).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean on vfio.sh AND the extracted watchdog; the repo harness extended to 144 checks (block 24: statics + a functional re-drive of the block-23 sandbox — the heal events land in the ring, the ring stays bounded at 20, and the --verbose run prints the history header + the event lines) — 144/144 PASS; the live-attach regression 595/595 green; --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R98 -> R99. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-27 19:53 UTC: The libvirt wedge now AUTO-HEALS, with notification — plus `--unstuck` and TUI menu option 14 in `vfio.sh` (R98 — the user requests: "add it and make it auto heal with notification so user dont have to do it by self" + "add to tui to with new settings that also confirm on menu if it is off or on same with looking glass stuff"):
  - feature 1 (the watchdog): `--install-dynamic-binding` now also installs a systemd timer (`vfio-libvirt-watchdog.timer`, every 2 minutes, oneshot) that runs the generated `/usr/local/sbin/vfio-libvirt-watchdog.sh`: (a) it KILLS any `vfio-libvirt-hook` process older than VFIO_WD_HUNG_SECS (default 90s — R97 bounds a healthy hook's calls to ~50s worst case, so a healthy hook is never touched; the awk match uses the `[k]` bracket trick so the watchdog's own ps line can never self-match) — the stuck libvirt job then completes, exactly step 1 of the manual recipe from the live incident; (b) it probes libvirt with a TIMEOUT-BOUNDED `virsh list --all` (VFIO_WD_PROBE_SECS, default 10) and, ONLY on a FAILED probe with the daemon claiming active, restarts virtqemud/libvirtd — safe because a daemon that fails the probe is not serving anything; (c) a cooldown (VFIO_WD_COOLDOWN, default 600s; stamp `autoheal-last.ts` in the dynamic state dir) prevents restart-flapping when the card is fully dead — that state needs the host reboot, and the notification says so (the R96 dead-GPU fallback boots the VM GPU-less meanwhile). Every action journal-logs (`vfio-dynamic` tag, `autoheal:`) and desktop-notifies (the doctor monitor's proven runuser + notify-send pattern, every active session). It NEVER acts on a healthy system (no hung hooks + responsive probe = silent exit) and NEVER restarts the daemon while the probe succeeds. The state dir honors VFIO_DYNAMIC_DIR (testable); the VFIO_WD_* values are tuning knobs.
  - feature 2 (`--unstuck`): the new CLI flag runs the watchdog script VERBOSELY right now — detect + heal a wedged libvirt immediately, with the actions printed; the manual recovery recipe from the R97 incident is now one command, and it doubles as the post-install live test (the installer's closing note says so). Falls back to an honest note + rc 1 if the watchdog is not installed.
  - feature 3 (TUI main-menu option 14): "Libvirt auto-heal watchdog (auto-heals a wedged libvirt + notification — every 2 minutes)" — context-aware exactly like option 13 (the LG auto-start pattern, per the request): it CONFIRMS the current state in the menu (timer ON and running / installed but inactive / not installed) and offers install / reinstall / remove, with the verdict popup explaining what was done + the `sudo vfio --unstuck` live-test tip. Exit moved 14 -> 15 (the R55/R69/R80 renumber precedent; no assertion locks the Exit index).
  - wiring: full CLI plumbing (parse_args, the MODE comment appended at the END so locked contiguous substrings stay intact, usage one-liner, help block, fish + bash + zsh completions — the completion-output regression re-run PASS with the new flag covered), `install_dynamic_binding` installs the watchdog (before the hook self-check), `--reset` and `--install-early-binding` sweep it (timer disable + file removal — the uninstaller rule), `--uninstall-self` notes that the watchdog STAYS (dynamic-binding infrastructure, like the hook), and the modprobe gate now also exempts `--unstuck`.
  - regression locks: 8 new R98 asserts in regression/live-attach-regression.sh (the installer, the bracket-trick kill pattern, the bounded probe, the cooldown stamp, the 2-minute timer, the --unstuck mode, the menu state confirmation, the reset sweep).
  - regression debt found + fixed while validating (the live-attach suite had not been re-run END-TO-END since the R77 era — the R94-R97 locks had never actually executed): the R95 stop-log lock expanded an unescaped `$_dom` inside DOUBLE quotes (set -u killed the whole suite at that assert), and the R94 patcher-defined lock called assert_contains_text with a FILE PATH (that helper greps the text it is given, so a path argument can never match — permanently red). Both fixed in lockstep; the full suite now runs end-to-end green (588 asserts, 0 FAIL, rc=0) with the 8 R98 locks verified live in the same run.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean on vfio.sh AND the extracted watchdog script; the repo harness extended to 134 checks (block 23: 8 statics + a FUNCTIONAL sandbox drive of the extracted watchdog — a failing fake virsh + an active fake systemctl + fake logger/notify-send/runuser prove the heal FIRES (the daemon restart logged + the cooldown stamp written), the still-dead verdict reaches the desktop notification, the journal line lands, and the COOLDOWN suppresses an immediate second heal) — 134/134 PASS; --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression PASS. Revision bumped R97 -> R98. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-27 19:26 UTC: Every libvirt call the hook makes is now TIMEOUT-BOUNDED in `vfio.sh` (R97 — the LIVE freeze report: "the vm freezes unable to close... virt manager frozen won't quit", root-caused live on the host):
  - the incident: after switching to dynamic binding (hotplug off), the VM froze at stop — the kernel journal shows the exact zombie chain: qemu's release-time reset on the card entered a `vfio_bar_restore: reset recovery` loop, then `Device config space inaccessible; unable to save state` on BOTH functions, then `pcieport: Data Link Layer Link Active not set` — the card fell off the bus mid-reset; qemu died stuck; virtqemud fell into an I/O-error loop against the dead qemu; and the R95/R96 hook's UNBOUNDED libvirt calls (`virsh domstate --reason` in the forensics, the synchronous `vfio --live-attach-off` restore) hung against the wedged daemon — TWO hung "stopped end" hook processes blocked libvirt's stop job, so `virsh destroy` and virt-manager queued forever.
  - the fix: the forensics reason call is now `timeout 5` (the reason is nice-to-have; on timeout it logs 'unknown' via the existing fallback), the R96 mode-restore CLI is `timeout 25` (the flag survives a timeout and retries at the next stop), and the dead-GPU fallback worker's on/start calls are `timeout 30` each. A hook can never again deepen a libvirt wedge. The R64/R96 rescue + notification logic is unchanged.
  - the live recovery recipe (worked on the host, recorded for the future): `sudo pkill -f vfio-libvirt-hook` (unblocks the stop job) → kill the frozen virt-manager client → `sudo systemctl restart virtqemud` (clears the stuck qemu connection) → verify with a timeout-wrapped `virsh list --all`. After the incident the card was fully off the bus (rescan failed) — the known host-reboot verdict; the R96 flow (deployed) handles the GPU-less boot + the automatic restore of the hotplug-off choice after the reboot.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean; the repo harness extended to 120 checks (120/120 PASS) with the new R97 block (the four timeout statics + a functional re-run of the forensics unit through the bounded call — the fake virsh returns instantly, the timeout passes through). --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R96 -> R97. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-27 19:03 UTC: The dead-GPU fallback is now MODE-PRESERVING in `vfio.sh` (R96 — the user request: "make it survive the flip — it still off by user choice; when it is rebooted give message to user 'now you can use hot attach because card is alive now'"):
  - feature 1 (the choice survives the flip): when the dead-GPU fallback rescues a VM start by flipping a hotplug-OFF VM to hotplug-on (the GPU-less boot — the VM is usable immediately, no virt-manager surgery), it FIRST writes a per-domain pending-restore flag (/var/lib/vfio-dynamic/restore-mode-off.<domain>) and says so in the rescue messages ("your hotplug-OFF choice is REMEMBERED — restored automatically once the GPU is healthy"). The flip is now explicitly a TEMPORARY rescue, not a silent config change.
  - feature 2 (the auto-restore): the hook's stopped phase checks the flag + the card's real liveness (new `_gpu_alive_now`: readable vendor + config head — the opposite of the all-0xff zombie). Once the card is healthy again (i.e. after the host reboot the zombie forced), the hook runs `vfio --live-attach-off` SYNCHRONOUSLY (fast — a define per VM; the flag clears only on success, so a failed restore retries at the next stop) and desktop-notifies: "GPU is alive again — your hotplug-OFF setting is restored; the next VM start cold-attaches the GPU as you configured."
  - feature 3 (the requested reboot message): the hook's prepare phase, when the flag exists AND the card is now alive (the first VM start after the reboot), immediately desktop-notifies the EXACT requested message: "The RX card is back on the bus — you can use hot-attach now. This session boots GPU-less once more; your hotplug-OFF setting is restored automatically when it ends." (The restore stays in the stopped phase: redefining the XML mid-prepare would not affect the already-snapshotted GPU-less start.) The hook gained its own best-effort `_notify_desktop` (the live-attach helper's proven runuser-to-/run/user/* pattern — it now exists in all 3 generated scripts).
  - feature 4 (the user always wins): a DELIBERATE toggle (`--live-attach-on/off`, tray) clears the pending-restore flag for every VM it successfully redefines — the user's explicit action always supersedes any pending auto-restore.
  - regression locks: 7 new R96 asserts in regression/live-attach-regression.sh (the flag write, the remembered-choice log, the restore action, the alive-again action, the liveness probe, the notifier, the toggle-clears-flag).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean; the repo harness extended to 115 checks (115/115 PASS) with the new R96 block. One pre-ship harness assert caught by its own run: the notifier count check expected 2 but the bind script ALSO defines `_notify_desktop` (3 total — corrected before shipping). --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R95 -> R96. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-27 18:52 UTC: Zombie forensics in the generated libvirt hook in `vfio.sh` (R95 — the accepted follow-up to the RX 9070 zombie root-cause dive, "ok do it"):
  - feature: the hook now stamps every VM-session start (per-domain `${VFIO_DYNAMIC_DIR:-/var/lib/vfio-dynamic}/vm-start.<domain>.ts` at the prepare phase) and logs `action=vm-stopped reason='<libvirt stop reason>' session=<n>s` at every ACTUAL VM stop — the new `_log_vm_stop_forensics` reads libvirt's own classification (`shut off (shutdown)` = the guest powered off gracefully, `shut off (destroyed)` = force-stop, `crashed`), gated to the 'stopped' phase so migration/managed-save 'release' events never log false stops. Root-cause context: the RX 9070 (Navi 48) release-time zombie wedges during the Windows guest driver's power-down path — the AMD "reset gap" (PCIe-level resets cannot reach the SMU/PSP firmware state; remove+rescan and even SBR restore the LINK but not the endpoint — verified live twice on this host, both zombies at VM-stop, AER silent, only host reboots recovered). After a few cycles, "which shutdown mode and which session lengths wedge the card" becomes hard journal data: `journalctl -t vfio-dynamic | grep vm-stopped`, then compare the reason/session of stops that zombied vs stops that didn't.
  - tooling: the consolidated R82-R94 validation harness had been WIPED from /tmp for the SECOND time this arc — it now lives in the repo as `regression/vm-tui-harness.sh` (durable, auto-chmodded, per-run mktemp scratch dir with an EXIT cleanup trap; the keep-tests-organized rule), extended from 99 to 107 checks with the new R95 block: statics (the stamp, the reason log, the phase gate) + the extracted whole-hook bash -n + a FUNCTIONAL unit of the extracted forensics logger (a fake virsh reason + a planted start-stamp 100s ago -> `reason='shut off (destroyed)'` + `session=<n>s` logged; without the stamp -> `session=unknown`, never fatal).
  - regression locks: 4 new R95 asserts added to regression/live-attach-regression.sh (the logger defined, the stamp line, the reason+session log line, the gated call).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean on vfio.sh AND the new harness (+ syntax check on the updated regression); the harness 107/107 PASS (all R82-R94 blocks re-verified after the relocation + the new R95 block). One pre-ship harness bug caught by its own bash -n before any run: an unquoted `<empty>` in a FAIL message parsed as a redirect — fixed. --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R94 -> R95. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-25 03:15 UTC: The Modify-VM "Set up Looking Glass" path now makes the whole chain seamless in `vfio.sh` (R94 — the user request: add the SPICE USB redirection channel needed for Looking Glass USB audio into the VM, and end the process by telling the user to install the Looking Glass Windows host):
  - feature 1 (the SPICE USB redirector): the LG setup now ENSURES a `<redirdev bus='usb' type='spicevmc'/>` device in every guest-GPU VM — the new idempotent python patcher `_lg_ensure_usb_redir_to_vm` (exit 3 when a redirector already exists; a virt-manager-created VM usually already has several, and the live XML of this host's GAMING VM showed 6). It is wired into BOTH apply paths: `install_looking_glass`'s per-VM pass (the Modify-VM "Set up Looking Glass" entry + `--install-looking-glass`) AND `_lg_apply_to_vm` (so the live-attach auto-LG path gets it too). The B7 USB-audio path needs it: the client's `spice:usbAudio` creates a virtual USB sound device in the guest THROUGH an unused redirector — Windows then shows "Looking Glass USB Audio" speakers (Sound settings), low-latency audio independent of the VM's emulated sound card. The setup's up-front description block lists the new step, and the per-VM output says whether one was added or already present.
  - feature 2 (the end-of-process final step): `install_looking_glass` now ENDS with the whole remaining chain spelled out — install the Looking Glass HOST application inside Windows (`looking-glass-host-setup.exe` matching the client version, from looking-glass.io; it installs the host service + the IVSHMEM driver, and from then on the shared memory initializes at every boot and the client connects automatically — verified live: without it every client window stays black), pick the "Looking Glass USB Audio" speakers in Windows Sound settings (enabled by the redirector added above), and the seamless auto-start tip (`sudo vfio --install-lg-autostart`). The `_install_looking_glass_defaults` (hotplug auto-LG) ending gained the same host-app one-liner.
  - regression locks: 7 new R94 asserts added to regression/live-attach-regression.sh (the patcher defined, the exact ET.Element append line, both call sites, the description line, the final-step host-app text, the USB-audio sound-setting text).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean (+ syntax checks on the updated regression + harness); the harness extended to 99 checks (99/99 PASS): statics (the patcher, both call sites, the final-step text, the USB-audio text, the defaults one-liner) + a FUNCTIONAL unit of the extracted patcher against a fake VM XML (a VM without a redirector gets one added; the patched XML re-run exits 3 — idempotent). --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R93 -> R94. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-25 03:11 UTC: Every host-app readiness check now accepts BOTH IVSHMEM magics in `vfio.sh` (R93 — verified LIVE after the user installed the Windows-side host app: the shared memory now carries the B7 LGMP session magic 4c 47 4d 50 06, and a manually-launched client connected to the REAL stream and displayed the Windows session):
  - root cause: the R73 readiness check looked for ONE magic — the legacy pre-B6 KVMFR header ("4b564d4652") — but the B7 host application writes an LGMP session header ("4c474d50" + version byte) into the first bytes of the IVSHMEM. The single-magic check therefore reported a RUNNING host app as "not initialized": the auto-start watcher never fired (it sat silent all day while the host app was up), the manager's host-app wait always timed out (the "expect a BLACK window" warnings), and the picker never showed the `screen ✔` marker — even though the client itself connected and displayed fine (it speaks LGMP directly).
  - fix: a shared vmtui_lg_magic_ok helper (vm-tui block) + a standalone twin inside the generated watcher accept BOTH magics; vmtui_lg_hoststate, vmtui_lg_shm_file's custom-name scan, the watcher's host_app_ready, and the watcher's shm_file scan all delegate to it. The manager's KVMFR wait, the `screen ✔` picker marker, VM_TUI_WAIT_CONNECT, and the auto-start watcher now all see the B7 host app as ready.
  - deployment note: the watcher is GENERATED — after --install-self, REGENERATE it (sudo vfio --install-lg-autostart, or main-menu option 13) so the deployed /usr/local/bin/vfio-lg-autostart picks up the dual-magic check; until then the running watcher still uses the old single-magic logic.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean; the /tmp harnesses had been WIPED — the R82 harness was REBUILT as a consolidated 92-check suite (all R82-R92 blocks reconstructed + the new R93 block): dual-magic statics, a functional unit of the extracted hoststate chain (KVMFR -> ready / LGMP -> ready / zeros -> not initialized), a functional unit of the watcher's standalone twin (LGMP -> ready / zeros -> not ready), the extracted watcher's bash -n, AND an END-TO-END drive of the full extracted watcher with a fake virsh (running) + an LGMP fake shm + a fake client proving the auto-start now FIRES on the B7 magic (the client launched + the launch logged). 92/92 PASS. --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). Revision bumped R92 -> R93. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.

- 2026-11-25 02:30 UTC: The agent-wait timeout WARN is now SELF-DIAGNOSING in `vfio.sh` (R89 — the accepted R88 follow-up suggestion, "ok do it as last" — the last change of this arc):
  - feature: new vmtui_agent_wait_diagnose runs ONE final guest-ping at the moment the agent wait times out (LC_ALL=C, so the virsh error text is locale-stable — the R77 lesson; this host's virsh speaks Swedish) and prints an exact verdict that is appended to the WARN ("Final check: ...") and mirrored into the agent-wait dbg event: the agent JUST answered (the wait missed it by a moment — reported as "Windows is up", NOT a WARN) / the channel exists but Windows has NOT connected the agent (still booting, or the agent service stopped/dropped) / NO agent channel in the VM (the earlier probes saw one) / no answer within 8s. The original WARN sentence is kept VERBATIM (additive); the generated-helper lock on "guest agent did not respond within" is untouched (it lives in the helper heredoc, not this WARN).
  - why (the open R87-session mystery): the manager's 120s agent wait timed out even though the live-attach helper caught the agent-lifecycle CONNECT at 11s, and a manual ping minutes later said "not connected" — Windows may drop the agent after the GPU hot-attach. The next live run now answers "still booting vs dropped" in the WARN itself, without opening the debug log; with debug on (option 9), the R84 agent-wait dbg events + the new diagnosis event pin the whole story.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean (+ the harness script's own syntax check); the R82 harness extended to 64 checks (64/64 PASS): statics (the helper, the LC_ALL=C final ping, the just-answered-is-OK-not-WARN case, the Final-check note, the dbg mirror) + a FUNCTIONAL unit of the extracted helper against a fake virsh with all four canned outcomes (just-answered rc 0 / not-configured / not-connected / silent — each with its exact verdict + rc asserted) + the fake-virsh call log proving the ping actually ran. --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). The R79 watcher harness not re-run (watcher code untouched). Revision bumped R88 -> R89. Other regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-25 02:22 UTC: --install-self is now drift-proof and session-safe in `vfio.sh` (R88 — the accepted R87 follow-up suggestion, "ok do it"):
  - feature 1 (drift visibility): every install now reads the previously deployed SCRIPT_REVISION and says exactly what it updates FROM and TO ("Deployed revision updated: R86 -> R88." / "already up to date — completions refreshed" / "fresh install"), plus the deployed revision in the dry-run note and the same-file refresh note — the live reports that traced to a STALE deployed copy (a pre-R75 binary producing phantom bugs) can no longer be missed.
  - feature 2 (atomic install): the old `cp -a` rewrote the SAME inode in place — a bash process executing the deployed copy (an open VM-manager session) reads a shifting file mid-run. The install now goes through write_file_atomic (temp file + rename = a NEW inode), so a running session's interpreter keeps reading its own stable copy; 0755 + root:root are set by the install itself, and the explicit chmod/chown lines are kept as belt-and-suspenders (the R48l pkexec ownership requirement — enforced twice rather than zero times).
  - feature 3 (running-instance guard): an ANCHORED pgrep ("$SELF_INSTALL_BIN" followed by a space or end-of-line, $$ excluded so running the deployed copy itself to refresh does not trigger it) warns loudly when vfio process(es) are still running from the deployed copy + says to exit and re-open them — warn-not-refuse, because the atomic write makes the overwrite harmless (running instances are merely stale until restarted). The anchor is deliberate: the vfio-lg-autostart watcher and vfio-hotplug-tray merely CONTAIN the path as a prefix and never match.
  - regression lockstep: ONE R34 lock updated per the R55/R61/R77 precedent (the "cp -a" install assert now expects the atomic write_file_atomic form); the chmod/chown/same-file/completion locks are untouched.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean (+ syntax checks on the updated regression + harness scripts); the R82 harness extended to 54 checks (54/54 PASS): the atomic-install + drift + guard statics, PLUS a functional anchor test (a deployed-binary-shaped process IS matched while a watcher-shaped prefix name is NOT) and a drift-extraction pipeline unit. --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). The R79 watcher harness not re-run (watcher code untouched); the root-requiring self-install smoke skipped per instruction (its behavioral checks are unchanged by the atomic form: the installed copy still exists + is executable + backed up on re-run). - 2026-11-25 02:21 UTC: The Looking Glass launch can no longer be KILLED by its own launch log in `vfio.sh` (R87 — the live report: "it succeeded start vm and hotplug but failed looking glass"; root-caused LIVE on the host with a python open-flag discrimination):
  - root cause (the R75 fix was itself arming a NEW client-killer on modern systemd distros): fs.protected_regular=2 (the systemd default) denies any >> open — bash appends always carry O_CREAT — on a FOREIGN-OWNED file in a sticky world-writable /tmp, EVEN FOR ROOT. The R75 chown "repair" makes /tmp/vm-tui.log fb-owned; the ROOT manager's own parent-side >>$log redirect was then EACCES-denied ("Atkomst nekas" at the exact launch lines), and a failed redirect ABORTS the launch before exec — the client died twice ("retry exhausted") on a perfectly healthy file that BOTH gates (root's test -w AND the R82 runuser'd test -w) called writable, because access(2)/test -w does not model the O_CREAT open. Verified live: root's O_APPEND-only open succeeds, the O_CREAT open is denied, root creates new /tmp files fine, and fb's own appends succeed — ONLY root-appending-to-fb-owned fails.
  - fix 1 (the launch redirect moved to the TARGET-USER side): the ROOT-path launch is now `runuser -u <user> -- env <session-env> VMTUI_LAUNCH_*=... bash -c '<child>'` where the child — running as exactly the user the gates validated — re-probes its own >> open, writes the launch header, and `exec setsid`s the client with its own redirect, plus an IN-CHILD /dev/null fallback: a log problem can NEVER kill the client. `exec setsid` keeps the process chain (runuser waits on it), so the liveness-pid semantics are unchanged; the VM_TUI_LG_ARGS word-split now happens inside the target-user shell.
  - fix 2 (both gates now probe the REAL open): the non-root gate replaced test -w with a subshell `( exec >>"$log" )` real-open probe (set -e safe); the root gate kept the R82 runuser'd test -w (harness lockstep) and ADDED a runuser'd real-open probe — the O_CREAT denial class is now visible BEFORE the launch.
  - fix 3 (fresh-boot logging): the chown moved AFTER the touch — on a MISSING log the old order had root's touch CREATE the file root-owned (the chown before it was a no-op), and the fb-side gate then correctly refused it: no logging on fresh boots.
  - bug caught + fixed by the round's own harness BEFORE shipping: the child's header printf had `$(id -un)` in the domain= slot (argument order) — the functional 12f check failed on the first run and the swap was applied before any redeploy.
  - open observation (the next run with debug logging on will pin it): the agent-lifecycle CONNECT the live-attach helper caught at 11s did NOT reach the manager's own agent wait (it timed out at the full 120s, and the agent was not connected at diagnosis time — the R84 agent-wait dbg event records the exact rc); the guest agent was also observed not-connected minutes later, so Windows may have dropped it after the GPU hot-attach.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` + the recurring literal-slip scan clean; the R82 harness extended to 46 checks (46/46 PASS): statics for the child-side redirect form, the in-child guarded fallback, both real-open probes, and the chown-after-touch order; plus a FUNCTIONAL run of the extracted child payload — a writable log receives the header + the client's output, and an UNWRITABLE (444) log still runs the client with its output sent to /dev/null (the never-kill-the-client guarantee proven). --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS (no CLI flags changed). - 2026-11-24 20:09 UTC: R77 parity for the vm-tui Looking Glass handoff in `vfio.sh` (R78 — the accepted R77 follow-up, "as last"): vmtui_wait_agent (the start-lg / `lg` Windows-readiness wait) is now EVENT-DRIVEN too, with a deliberate design twist the helper did not need:
  - ONE quick probe guest-ping runs FIRST — it alone preserves the R70 semantics: instant rc 0 when the agent is already up (the common already-running VM case: `vfio --vm-tui start-lg` on a running VM), and the instant rc 2 "not configured" fast-path when the VM has NO agent channel. That twist was a REAL regression risk caught in design: an event-first approach (subscribe immediately, like the helper) would silently eat the whole budget on a channel-less VM, because no agent-lifecycle event can ever fire without a channel — the R70 "launch immediately + hint" path would have become a full-budget wait.
  - While the agent is booting (channel present, the probe says not-yet-connected): subscribe to libvirt's agent-lifecycle event (push — the exact connect moment, no 1s poll granularity, the same LC_ALL=C locale-proof connect marker as R77) for the REMAINING wall-clock budget; if the subscription cannot deliver a connect (old libvirt / timeout), the R70 poll loop runs VERBATIM as the fallback. Same return contract (0 = agent up, 2 = no channel, 1 = timeout) — every caller (act_start_lg's "Windows is up" / "No guest agent channel" / timeout-WARN notes) is unchanged.
  - bugs caught + fixed during the round (the recurring class, caught by the tooling before shipping): three more $-slips — the new function compared the LITERAL strings "_out" (twice, SC2193 flagged by shellcheck as can-never-be-equal) which would have made the probe ALWAYS take the not-configured/event paths; fixed and re-verified.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; a focused 13-check harness extracted vmtui_wait_agent VERBATIM and drove it with a fake virsh through all five scenarios (13/13 PASS): ready -> instant rc 0 with ZERO event calls; no-channel -> instant rc 2 with ZERO event calls (the design-critical case); boot-delay -> rc 0 via the CONNECTED event with exactly ONE probe ping (no poll spam); event-unsupported -> rc 0 via the poll fallback; event-timeout + agent absent -> honest rc 1. --self-test rc=0; the completion-output regression re-run PASS. - 2026-11-24 20:04 UTC: EVENT-DRIVEN live-attach readiness in `vfio.sh` (R77 — "add polish": the user asked for something that REPLACES the guest-ping polling for the GPU hotplug):
  - feature: the generated live-attach helper now FIRST subscribes to libvirt's **agent-lifecycle event** — libvirt PUSHES the event the instant the virtio-win guest agent connects to its virtio-serial channel, so the bind + hot-attach fires at the EXACT connect moment: no 3s poll granularity, no repeated 5s `virsh qemu-agent-command` round-trips, and the attach typically lands a few seconds EARLIER than the first successful poll. LC_ALL=C pins the event text to English so the `connect` marker is greppable regardless of the host locale (this host's virsh speaks Swedish); one event = the agent just connected (the channel starts disconnected at VM start, a disconnect event can only follow a connect). Fallback chain: event subscription unavailable / libvirt too old for `--event agent-lifecycle` / times out without a connect -> the R23 guest-ping poll loop VERBATIM (kept unchanged, wall-clock bounded, with the REMAINING budget) -> the full fixed delay — so every locked regression string survives and no scenario regresses.
  - honest scope (re-verified live before building it): the event replaces the POLLING, not the agent — the virtio-win guest agent must still be installed inside Windows (virtio-win-guest-tools.exe) for either signal to fire; this host's VM currently has the channel in the XML but the agent NOT installed (state=disconnected, live guest-ping fails, journal shows the full-30s fallback every boot). And truly instant attach at VM power-on remains deliberately unsupported — that is cold-attach, the PCIe-reset path that kills the RX 9070/RDNA4; the wait IS the protection on this card. Redeploy the updated helper: `sudo vfio.sh --install-live-attach`.
  - regression debt found + fixed while validating (the affected suite was run and 5 PRE-EXISTING failures surfaced — proven pre-existing by running the suite against the unmodified HEAD archive, NOT caused by R77): three MODE-comment locks (R27/R33/R34) were reflowed by R69's intentional `vm-tui` insertion, and two R48h locks still required `_lg_binary_valid "${LG_CLIENT_BIN` in the disclaimer/checklist after R68's intentional refactor to the `_lg_client_available` wrapper. All five updated IN LOCKSTEP with the intentional changes (the established R55/R61 pattern), and 5 new R77 asserts added (the event subscription, the LC_ALL=C pin, the connect-moment bind, the fallback line, the remaining-budget computation).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on vfio.sh AND on the extracted generated helper (494 lines); a functional sandbox harness extracted the readiness block VERBATIM and drove it with a fake virsh through all three paths (10/10 PASS: CONNECTED event -> ready with ZERO guest-ping calls — the whole point; event-unsupported -> the poll fallback still lands; event-timeout + agent absent -> the unchanged fixed-delay behavior); the affected regression suite re-run GREEN END-TO-END (559 asserts, 0 FAIL, rc=0). Runtime capture state cannot be queried from outside the client (no IPC), so the honest preset report + the flag pre-validation + the connection check are the strongest verifiable chain.
  - (4) MRU staleness — VERDICT: partially real. NOT real: the picker already filtered deleted VMs out of the rendered list (the R70 reorder only includes recents that exist), so there were never broken menu entries. REAL: the recent-vms file kept stale names forever, and a deleted last-VM was still offered as the ENTER default (and always failed to match). Fixed: the MRU list is pruned of names not in the live VM list at pick time (rewritten in MRU order, capped, reported to stderr), and the ENTER default is only offered when that VM still exists.
  - (5) start-lg recovery — VERDICT: half already done (start-lg on an ALREADY-RUNNING VM already degrades to the lg flow: the start is skipped, the wait + launch still run — that claim was not real); the real gap was no retry. Fixed: the launch now retries ONCE (VM_TUI_LG_RETRIES, default 1, 0 = off) when the client DIES early — the common transient first-launch race — logged explicitly ("retrying in 3s"), then the full diagnosis block. The retry fires ONLY on an early exit, never while a client is alive, so there is no double-window risk.
  - (6) session env — VERDICT: a real residual that cannot be fully eliminated (sandboxed compositors etc.). Hardened: the resolved WAYLAND_DISPLAY is now VALIDATED (absolute path directly; a relative name under XDG_RUNTIME_DIR — the wayland resolution rule); a STALE value (dead session env / broken SSH forward) triggers the live-socket scan fallback and, when nothing is live, a PRECISE refusal ("a stale WAYLAND_DISPLAY is not a display") instead of launching a client that would die with "could not connect to display". Both the non-root inherited env and the root session-env path validate identically. X11 DISPLAY cannot be validated from the shell and is accepted as-is (the liveness/connection checks guard it).
  - accepted R71 follow-up suggestions: the root main-menu entry (option 12) now PRE-WARNS when no desktop session is detectable (a QUIET probe — VMTUI_LG_QUIET — of the SAME resolver, one code path, no drift) so the operator knows BEFORE picking a VM that the LG launch will refuse; and the bounded retry (point 5).

- 2026-11-18 21:10 UTC: Fixed the VFIO kernel health audit grading a perfectly healthy boot as HEALTH: FAIL / CRITICAL in `vfio.sh` (R65: log-scan false positive on the kernel command-line echo):
  - user report (live): the wizard's "full configure with recommended defaults" run printed `CRITICAL: Detected vfio-pci probe/BAR errors in kernel logs that match this GPU/kernel` and `HEALTH: FAIL`, with the two "log matches" being the kernel COMMAND-LINE ECHO lines — while the same audit simultaneously said `VFIO environment OK (IOMMU, vfio-pci, no framebuffer lock): YES`.
  - root cause: the log scanner's filter chain (`grep -i "vfio-pci" | grep -Ei "(BAR|cannot reserve|can't reserve|probe|failed to (enable|assign))" | grep -i "$dmesg_filter"`) matched the cmdline echo because the cmdline contains the literal strings "vfio-pci" (from this script's OWN `vfio-pci.disable_idle_d3=1` param) and "bar" (from this script's OWN `amdgpu.rebar=0` param — "re**bar**" matches the case-insensitive `BAR`). Every boot with this script's recommended params would false-positive.
  - fix (audit_vfio_health): (1) kernel command-line echo lines are excluded BEFORE the signature greps (`grep -vi "command line:"`) — they are informational, never errors, and panic dumps that echo the cmdline are already handled by the dedicated real-panic signature check; (2) "BAR" now requires a BAR index (`BAR [0-9]` — real messages read "BAR 1: can't reserve ..."), so `amdgpu.rebar=0` can never match even outside echo lines; (3) "probe" now requires a failure word on the same line (`probe.*fail|fail.*probe` — real messages read "probe of <dev> failed"), so successful-probe lines can never match.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; LIVE before/after proof on the reporting host against the SAME current-boot journal: the old pipeline matched exactly the 2 cmdline echo lines (the reported false positive, reproduced), the fixed pipeline matches nothing; end-to-end `bash vfio.sh --health-check` after the fix: no CRITICAL line, verdict `Kernel regression: NO (no vfio-pci BAR/probe failures found for this GPU)` — the remaining WARN is the GENUINE dead-GPU notice (the card is off the bus pending the host reboot), not a log-scan artifact. No regression suite locks the old pattern (verified by grep — the custom-kernel-params assertions lock the rebar PARAM being added, not the audit scanner). Revision bumped R64 -> R65. Regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.
- 2026-11-18 20:47 UTC: A dead guest GPU can no longer block the VM — dead-GPU resilience with a GPU-less auto-fallback in `vfio.sh` (R64: unified actionable abort + exit-42 protocol + live-attach fallback + auto-revive hot-attach):
  - user request (live): starting VM 'GAMING' failed with `Körning av hook-skriptet misslyckades ... ERROR: Guest GPU not present in sysfs: 0000:0e:00.0` (the libvirt hook aborted because the RX 9070 dropped off the PCI bus entirely — the zombie state). The user asked that the VM be startable anyway ("they should able start vm bespide with concidaraation"), that the message tell the user what happened and to reboot when their work is done, that the GPU auto-disable (VM runs without it) and auto-enable when alive again — "make sure it is stupid and bullet proof concept".
  - root cause of the poor UX: the OLD bind-script top-level check `[[ -d sysfs ]] || die "Guest GPU not present in sysfs"` ran BEFORE argument parsing — no recovery attempt, no guidance beyond the terse die, and a VM start that failed outright (the exact live report).
  - fix (bind script, unified abort): new `_dead_gpu_abort <ctx> [bdf]` prints ONE consistent, actionable story on every dead-GPU path — what happened (the card dropped off the bus; the 'zombie' state qemu reports as 'Unknown PCI header type 127'), that it is NOT a configuration problem (verify the BDF if hardware changed), the REAL fix (save your work and reboot the host when convenient), and that the VM start is NOT lost (the fallback restarts it without the GPU) — plus a desktop notification, and exits with the DEDICATED code 42 (the hook's fallback trigger). All four dead-GPU abort sites route through it: the new top-level gate, the cooldown readiness probe, and both do_bind alive-check aborts.
  - fix (bind script, dead-GPU gate): the old pre-arg-parse sysfs die is replaced by a gate AFTER argument parsing — a bare PCI rescan is attempted first (a remove is impossible with no sysfs entry; a rescan can re-enumerate the card); if the card returns, the bind proceeds normally; if not, --bind-now exits 42 via _dead_gpu_abort, --release tolerates it (records the cooldown timestamp, exits 0 — a dead card must not fail the VM-stop flow), and the early-binding boot path keeps the old loud die (a dead card at boot IS a reboot case).
  - fix (hook, GPU-less fallback): new `_dead_gpu_fallback` in the libvirt hook — when --bind-now exits 42 and `VFIO_DEAD_GPU_FALLBACK=1` (default), the hook prints the fallback story (GPU dead / host reboot required / VM restarting without the GPU / live-attach stays on until toggled off), stamps a loop-guard file, and spawns a FULLY DETACHED worker (setsid, off the libvirt pipe, after the hook exits — no re-entrancy on the hook script itself) that flips the VM into live-attach mode=on (`vfio --live-attach-on`: GPU stripped from the persistent XML, fragment saved) and auto-restarts it via `virsh start` seconds later. Net operator experience: click Start -> one clear error dialog + a desktop notification explaining everything -> the VM comes up anyway on the virtual display a few seconds later. LOOP-GUARDED: one fallback per domain per 10 minutes (stamp cleared on the next successful bind, auto-expires), so a missing CLI/virsh or a still-dead card can never cause an auto-restart loop. `VFIO_DEAD_GPU_FALLBACK=0` restores the old fail-the-start behavior.
  - fix (helper, auto-revive): the live-attach helper now waits for the GPU to be ALIVE before binding — `_gpu_alive` + a bounded poll (`VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_GPU_ALIVE_WAIT`, default 120s, 0 = old fail-fast): if the card is dead when the helper runs (the fallback just started the VM GPU-less), it notifies the operator, polls, and binds + hot-attaches the MOMENT the card is back (the park-keepalive recovery or the operator's host reboot + VM restart). If it never revives within the window, the helper exits with a clear "VM keeps running WITHOUT the GPU; save your work and reboot" notification. The VM is never blocked — the helper is detached.
  - wiring: new conf keys `VFIO_DEAD_GPU_FALLBACK="1"` and `VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_GPU_ALIVE_WAIT="120"` (both with full WHY notes in the generated conf; the scripts default correctly on existing confs without them); revision bumped R63 -> R64; a successful --bind-now clears the loop-guard stamp (both hook dispatch sites).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on vfio.sh AND on all three extracted GENERATED scripts (bind 1487 lines / hook 324 / helper 473 — the same extraction the regression smoke uses); a 23-check sandbox: the LIVE dead-GPU gate test ran the extracted bind script against the GENUINELY dead card on this host (the exact reported scenario, safe as non-root): rescan attempted, exit 42, full actionable message; the hook fallback sandbox (fake bind/vfio/virsh/hp farm) proved: (A) rc 42 -> the fallback story, (B) the detached worker really calls `--live-attach-on` + `virsh start GAMING`, (C) the immediate retry is loop-guarded (no second worker), (D) a non-42 failure (rc 1) still aborts plainly, (E) `VFIO_DEAD_GPU_FALLBACK=0` opts out with no worker; all four regression-locked strings re-verified intact ('probing card readiness' / both 'card needs a host reboot' variants / 'action=bind-now-failed rc=$_rc') so the existing suites stay green. Regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.

- 2026-11-17 19:38 UTC: Fixed the python3 tray applet crashing with SIGABRT at tray-install time in `vfio.sh` (R61: display-less launch fix + session-env discovery + notify-send auto-install):
  - user request: "ok do and also do it but looks att python 3 crashes beacuse it crash when tries to enable tray icon" — do the R60 follow-up (notify-send auto-install) AND fix the observed python3 crash when the tray icon tries to start.
  - live investigation (journal + coredumpctl + a controlled manual run): TWO `/usr/bin/python3.13` SIGABRT coredumps at 20:25:36/20:25:55 local (exactly tray-install time), cmdline `python3 /usr/local/bin/vfio-hotplug-tray` launched via `runuser` from the sudo install. Qt output: `could not connect to display` (an EMPTY display) -> `Could not load the Qt platform plugin "xcb"` -> qFatal abort. ROOT CAUSE: the install-time launch inherited `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`/`DISPLAY` from the sudo/root environment — which does NOT have them (sudo env_reset strips them) — so the applet started display-less, Qt fell back to the default xcb platform with an empty DISPLAY, and aborted. A manual in-session launch (`WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-1`) runs fine (stayed up under an 8s probe), proving the applet code was healthy.
  - diagnostic honesty note (initially wrong premise, refuted and REVERTED): the journal also printed `From 6.5.0, xcb-cursor0 or libxcb-cursor0 is needed to load the Qt xcb platform plugin`, which initially looked like a missing `libxcb-cursor0` — an `ensure_qt_xcb_cursor` auto-installer was written on that premise and then REMOVED after `rpm -q libxcb-cursor0` showed the lib installed since 2026-11-14 (3 days BEFORE the crash): the line is Qt's canned plugin-failure advisory, not a missing library. Recorded here so the history stays honest.
  - fix (launch env): new `_tray_session_display_env <user>` discovers the DESKTOP session's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`/`DISPLAY` from the user's live session process environ (kwin_wayland / plasmashell / gnome-shell / sway / hyprland / Xorg / wayfire / labwc / river / niri — picks up non-default sockets like wayland-1; root can read another user's /proc environ, a user its own), falling back to the standard wayland sockets in /run/user/<uid>. The install-time launch now builds an env array (XDG_RUNTIME_DIR + the discovered vars, then the inherited env as fallback) for the `setsid runuser` detach, and both the Launched and WARN notes print the effective launch env for diagnosability.
  - fix (applet hardening): the generated tray applet pre-checks for a display (`WAYLAND_DISPLAY` / `DISPLAY` / `QT_QPA_PLATFORM`) before creating QApplication and exits CLEANLY (rc=1) with an actionable stderr message + a log line to /tmp/vfio-hotplug-tray.log instead of a Qt qFatal abort/coredump — the autostart entry still starts it inside the session on login.
  - feature (the "ok do" part): new `ensure_notify_send` auto-installs notify-send when missing (zypper: `libnotify-tools` — openSUSE ships notify-send there, NOT in libnotify / dnf+yum: `libnotify` / apt: `libnotify-bin` / pacman+apk+xbps: `libnotify`; verified online) — PURELY BEST-EFFORT with the `have_cmd notify-send` re-check as the success criterion; every notify-send call in the applet is try/except, so a failure never blocks or skips the tray (a note with the manual command is printed instead).
  - wiring (per the version + uninstaller rules): revision bumped R60 -> R61 (header + SCRIPT_REVISION + revision notes); `--self-test` gained an informational `notify-send (notifications)` line; `--uninstall-self` documents that an auto-installed libnotify package is a shared system package deliberately NOT auto-removed; the R42 setsid-detach assertion in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` updated in lockstep for the new env-array launch form (`env "${_env_args[@]}"`).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on vfio.sh AND the updated regression script; an 18-check sandbox harness (fake sudo/zypper farms, hermetic per-scenario PATHs/dirs, /tmp only, no real packages, no root): LIVE `_tray_session_display_env` returns the real session env (DISPLAY=:0 WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-1 read from the live session process), ensure_notify_send success (correct `sudo zypper --non-interactive in libnotify-tools` logged) + DRY_RUN (note, no PM call), the smoke heredoc extraction + py_compile, and THE CRASH-FIX PROOF: the regenerated applet run display-less (`env -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY -u DISPLAY -u QT_QPA_PLATFORM`) exits cleanly rc=1 with the actionable message and logs the refusal — exactly the state that SIGABRT'd (rc=134) before; static wiring asserts (R61 revision, session-env discovery + env-array launch wired, applet pre-check present, notify-send line, uninstaller note, regression lockstep, and the false-premise xcb-cursor symbols fully removed). Live read-only `bash vfio.sh --self-test` clean: `PySide6 (tray toolkit): OK`, `zenity (tray dialogs): OK`, `notify-send (notifications): OK`. Regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.

The TUI previously required exactly `whiptail`, which is not a package name on Fedora/RHEL (newt provides it) and is absent from openSUSE Leap / Void — a fresh distro silently fell back to plain-text with no hint and no remedy.
  - feature (dual backend): new `TUI_BIN` (whiptail preferred, dialog fallback) set by the new `tui_backend_detect()`; all 7 direct whiptail call sites (prompt_yn --yesno x2, confirm_phrase --inputbox, gui_msgbox --msgbox, _verdict_popup --msgbox, select_from_list --menu, the openSUSE remove-default-kernel --inputbox) now invoke `"$TUI_BIN"`, so either backend renders every dialog. Compatibility verified EMPIRICALLY before the change: in a pty, `whiptail` and `dialog` under the existing `3>&1 1>&2 2>&3` fd dance both captured exactly `CAPTURED_RESULT=[DEFAULTTXT] rc=0` — dialog(1) writes its RESULT to stderr by default ("Normally that is the standard error"), the same contract whiptail uses, so the shared dance is unchanged for both backends.
  - feature (auto-install, NO prompts): new `ensure_tui_backend()` gated by the new `_mode_needs_tui_backend()` allowlist (install/menu/reset/install-self/uninstall-self + every interactive install-*/switch/remove mode; read-only + machine modes — verify, detect, doctor, self-test, completion printers, status reports — NEVER install packages). When no backend is present it installs the distro package list in order until one succeeds: apt-get whiptail->dialog (plus an apt-get update + retry pass for fresh images with empty package lists), dnf/yum newt->dialog (Fedora/RHEL: the newt package PROVIDES whiptail), zypper newt->dialog, pacman whiptail->libnewt->dialog, apk newt->dialog, xbps-install dialog->newt. Gate placement in main() after the dependency checks, ONLY when already root — a non-root run is re-exec'd under sudo by require_root in the mode dispatch, and the root instance then runs the same gate (no double sudo password prompts). `--no-tui` (new `TUI_DISABLED_BY_FLAG`) and `--dry-run` skip the auto-install entirely.
  - failure handling (never fatal — the "do something about that part" request): offline / repo error / package unavailable / no package manager / no root+sudo -> prints what was attempted + the EXACT manual install command for the detected package manager (or the per-distro list when no manager is found) and continues in plain-text mode — every prompt still works (numbered menus, y/n questions). Verified in the sandbox (fake PATH farms): a failing fake dnf ran newt-then-dialog in order + printed the fallback notes + the Fedora hint while HAS_TUI stayed 0; a no-package-manager run printed the no-PM branch with the per-distro manual commands; both runs survived `set -euo pipefail`.
  - uninstaller note (per the uninstaller rule): `--uninstall-self` now documents that the newt/whiptail/dialog package the script may have auto-installed is a SHARED system package (debconf on Debian, NetworkManager-tui on Alpine, ...) and is deliberately NOT auto-removed — with the manual removal command per package manager (dnf remove newt / apt-get remove whiptail / zypper rm newt / pacman -R whiptail / apk del newt / xbps-remove dialog). `--reset` likewise never removes distro packages.

- 2026-10-30 20:37 UTC: Fixed the self-installed `vfio` CLI dying with "ERROR: Missing required command: modprobe" for non-root users in `vfio.sh` (R57: PATH hardening — append the sbin dirs when missing):
  - bug (live, user-reported): after `sudo ./vfio.sh --install-self`, running bare `vfio` as the normal user aborted at main()'s dependency gate with `ERROR: Missing required command: modprobe` (rc=1) even though modprobe IS installed (`/usr/sbin/modprobe` -> kmod). Root cause: on Debian-family systems modprobe (and friends: runuser, modinfo, ...) live in /usr/sbin & /sbin, which are NOT in the default PATH of regular user shells (observed live on this host: SparkyLinux + fish + linuxbrew — PATH = /home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/games:/snap/bin, NO sbin dirs; the bash PATH is identical). The install itself worked because sudo runs with root's PATH (which HAS the sbin dirs); only the non-root `vfio` invocation died — `need_cmd` uses `command -v`, which only searches PATH.
  - fix (R57 PATH hardening): immediately after SCRIPT_REVISION — BEFORE any need_cmd/have_cmd/`command -v` check — the script now appends the standard sbin dirs (/usr/local/sbin, /usr/sbin, /sbin) to PATH when missing AND the dir exists, then re-exports PATH. This fixes BOTH the dependency-gate resolution AND every later by-name invocation (`run modprobe ...`, `have_cmd runuser`, `modinfo`, ...) for non-root callers, and is a no-op for root (its PATH already contains the dirs). Plain `if [[ ":${PATH}:" != *":/sbin:"* && -d /sbin ]]` guards — set -e/-u safe, shellcheck-clean, no loop variables (avoids collision with the existing `_vfio_p` usage).
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on vfio.sh; LIVE A/B proof as the non-root user with a sbin-less PATH (env PATH=/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin): the OLD deployed R56 `--doctor` died with the exact reported 1-line error, while the fixed R57 completes the full 141-line read-only doctor report (modprobe gate passed; the rc=1 ATTENTION verdict is only the pre-existing "no /etc/vfio-gpu-passthrough.conf yet" state on this host — every other check PASS). Deployed to /usr/local/bin/vfio (root:root 0755, byte-identical to repo) via `sudo ./vfio.sh --install-self`; the deployed copy was re-verified with the same sbin-less non-root doctor run. Regression/smoke suites skipped per instruction.

- 2026-09-26 23:35 UTC: Fixed the live-attach tray toggle failing with rc=127 in `vfio.sh` (R48l: `install_self` now chowns the CLI `root:root` so pkexec accepts it):
  - bug (tray toggle rc=127): clicking the system-tray hotplug toggle ran `pkexec /usr/local/bin/vfio --live-attach-toggle` but pkexec returned rc=127 ("Not authorized" / command not found), so the toggle never worked from the tray. Root cause: `install_self` did `cp -a "$src" "$SELF_INSTALL_BIN"` + `chmod 0755` but NEVER `chown root:root`. `cp -a` preserves the source file's ownership, so when the operator ran `sudo ./vfio.sh --install-self` the installed CLI inherited the user's `fb:fb` ownership. pkexec (a security boundary) REFUSES to execute a binary that is not owned by root — so the tray applet's `pkexec /usr/local/bin/vfio --live-attach-toggle` was refused with rc=127 every time. The tray applet already called the absolute path (`VFIO_BIN = "/usr/local/bin/vfio"`), so this was NOT a PATH issue (pkexec's secure PATH excludes `/usr/local/bin`, but the absolute path bypasses that) — it was purely the ownership check.
  - fix: `install_self` now runs `chown root:root "$SELF_INSTALL_BIN"` after the `cp -a` + `chmod`, so the installed CLI is root-owned and pkexec accepts it. The `2>/dev/null || true` keeps it non-fatal on systems where chown is unavailable. Live-fix: `sudo chown root:root /usr/local/bin/vfio` applied to the already-installed binary so the tray toggle works now without a reinstall.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; full 16-suite `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 — 22 steps PASS, 0 FAIL. 1 new R48l assertion in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` (install_self chowns the CLI root:root). Release notes at `/home/fb/VFIO-passthrough-release-2026-09-26.md` updated.

  - R48j (Fedora build deps before source compile + build log on failure): the dnf branch's COPR-package-failed fallback jumped straight to `_lg_compile_from_source` with NO Fedora build deps installed (the apt branch installed deps first; the dnf branch did not), so the clone + cmake + make died on missing headers and the binary was never produced. Also `_lg_compile_from_source` fully silenced every step (`> /dev/null 2>&1`), so the user only ever saw "build failed, check dependencies" with NO clue which header/command was missing. Fixed: the official Looking Glass wiki Fedora client-build dependency list is now installed BEFORE the compile fallback; the clone/cmake/make output is captured to `$LG_BUILD_LOG` (`/tmp/looking-glass-client-build.log`) and the tail (last 40 lines) is printed on failure so the operator sees the REAL error (missing header, linker failure, ...). New `LG_BUILD_LOG` constant + `_lg_dump_log_tail` helper.
  - R48j-fix (live progress + exit-code return): root cause of the silent failure — `_lg_compile_from_source` returned 0/1 via `printf '0'`/`printf '1'` on STDOUT, and the caller did `_ok=$(_lg_compile_from_source)` which captures ALL of stdout, so every `note()` + the build output + the log tail were SWALLOWED into `$_ok` and never reached the terminal (the user saw nothing for minutes, then just the final error). Now the function returns via EXIT CODE, freeing stdout for live diagnostics. The clone/cmake/make output is tee'd to BOTH the terminal (live progress — git's clone %, cmake config, make compiling each file) AND `$LG_BUILD_LOG` (failure tail preserved); `--progress` added to git clone; step indicators `Step 1/4` … `Step 4/4` printed before each step. The dnf/apt deps install also shows the package manager's own download progress bar (removed the `>/dev/null 2>&1` that hid it).
  - R48j-cross (openSUSE / Void / Gentoo build-dep branches + Arch alignment): the compile fallback previously only handled dnf / pacman / apt — openSUSE, Void, and Gentoo users got "no recognized package manager" and a manual-install hint. Added the official Looking Glass wiki build-dependency lists for openSUSE (zypper), Void (xbps-install), and Gentoo (emerge), and aligned the Arch (pacman) list with the wiki (`libgl libegl` instead of `sdl2`; + `ttf-dejavu` + `libsamplerate` for audio). Each branch shows the package manager's own download/build progress live and then calls the shared `_lg_compile_from_source` (exit-code return) so diagnostics reach the terminal. The fallback error message now names all six package managers: `dnf/pacman/apt/zypper/xbps-install/emerge`.
  - R48j-selfheal (auto-install missing deps + cmake retry): the bleeding-edge Looking Glass master `CMakeLists` requires `fuse3>=3.10` (via `pkg_check_modules`), which was NOT in any of the up-front dep lists, so cmake configure died with `The following required packages were not found: - fuse3>=3.10` and the build aborted. Fixed two layers: (1) `fuse3` is now in EVERY distro's up-front dep list (dnf `fuse3-devel`, pacman `fuse3`, apt `libfuse3-dev`, zypper `fuse3-devel`, xbps `fuse3-devel`, emerge `sys-fs/fuse:3`) so the known gap is closed before the build starts; (2) cmake is now SELF-HEALING — on a missing pkg-config module failure, `_lg_cmake_with_retry` parses the `The following required packages were not found:` error block (via `_lg_extract_missing_modules`, stripping version constraints like `fuse3>=3.10` -> `fuse3`), maps each module to the distro package via a 20+ entry table `_lg_pkg_to_distro_pkg` (fontconfig, fuse3, libpipewire-0.3, libpulse, samplerate, nettle, gmp, spice-protocol, xkbcommon, wayland-*, libdecor, x11/xfixes/xi/xinerama/xscrnsaver/xcursor/xpresent/xrandr, gl/egl/glesv2, libusbredirparser — each mapped for all 6 package managers), auto-installs them via `_lg_install_pkgs`, and retries (caps at 3 attempts so a non-missing-module failure or an unmappable module does not loop forever). So the `fuse3>=3.10` failure now prints `cmake configure failed (attempt 1/3); missing pkg-config module(s): fuse3` -> `Auto-installing missing build dependency(ies) via dnf: fuse3-devel` -> installs it -> `Retrying cmake configure (attempt 2/3)...` and the build continues. Each distro branch sets a `_lg_pm` global so the retry helper knows which package manager to use.
  - R48k (compact Modify VM TUI menu): the flat 23-option `--menu` was hard to scan. Compacted to a 12-option top-level menu (Full configure ×2, **Modify VM**, Switch dynamic/early binding, Verify, Detect, Reset, Install/Uninstall self, Show VFIO status, Exit=11) with one prominent **Modify VM** entry that opens a sub-menu hub `_menu_modify_vm` with three categories: **Apply to VM** (`_menu_apply_to_vm` — every install action: hypervisor hide + stealth/perf, ultimate-perf, Looking Glass host setup, looking-glass-client compile, virtio-win guest-agent ISO, live-attach + toggle), **Revert changes on VM** (`_menu_revert_on_vm` — every revert/remove action: revert stealth/perf, revert ultimate-perf, remove Looking Glass, remove looking-glass-client, remove live-attach), and **Show VFIO status** (the per-VM checklist + ReBAR, now reachable from inside the Modify VM hub). The old flat VM entries (options 3/5–10/16–20) are gone; the status panel + ReBAR disclaimer + post-action status block + `Returning to menu` all still render.
  - R48k follow-up (no-config VM detection + BOTH-modes customizations + GPU hook checkmark): when the guard finds no `$CONF_FILE`, instead of just refusing, it offers to DETECT the operator's guest-GPU VM from libvirt (scans every libvirt domain for a PCI hostdev whose source is a display controller, class 0x03) and asks "is this your guest-GPU VM?" (or lets the operator pick from multiple candidates), then writes a MINIMAL conf (`GUEST_GPU_BDF` + `GUEST_GPU_VENDOR_ID` + `GUEST_GPU_DEVICE_ID` + `VFIO_BINDING_MODE=early`) from the pick — so VM customizations work in EARLY binding WITHOUT running the full wizard first. Helpers `_menu_detect_guest_gpu_vm_candidates` + `_menu_offer_minimal_conf_from_vm`. The guard's comment now states VM customizations "apply regardless of whether dynamic binding or the dynamic hook is present" — only live-attach + its toggle stay DYNAMIC-only (they rely on the libvirt qemu hook). So stealth/perf/Looking Glass/virtio-win/client run in early binding just fine. A shared `_menu_vm_guarded` helper enforces the conf + libvirt + binding-mode gate. A new `_menu_gpu_hook_status` helper prints `GPU hook: ✔ enabled (libvirt qemu hook installed)` / `✖ not installed (early binding, or hook missing)` in the Modify VM hub header AND both sub-menu headers so the operator sees at a glance whether the hook is applied. The top-level Show-VFIO-status option + the hub status option + the main-menu header now share one `_menu_build_vfio_status_block` builder so all three render the identical panel.

Host-level (one hook for all VMs), computed once outside the per-VM loop — same pattern as the looking-glass-client `compiled` check, and same source of truth as the `_menu_gpu_hook_status` header line so the checklist + the header always agree. Line 2 is now `vBIOS ✖  live-attach ✔  hugepages ✖  virtio-win ✖  disks-virtio ✔  hooked-gpu ✔`.

- 2026-09-03 19:35 UTC: Fixed hotplug breaking after enabling performance tuning in `vfio.sh` (R48i: boot-display re-pinned off the GPU reserved guest PCI bus on every live-attach VM define — the `XML error: Attempt to double-use PCI address 0000:06:00.0` root cause):
  - bug (hotplug dead after perf tuning): the user enabled ultimate-performance tuning and the live-attach GPU hot-attach then failed with the libvirt journal error `XML-fel: Försök att dubbelanvända PCI-adressen 0000:06:00.0` ("XML error: Attempt to double-use PCI address 0000:06:00.0"). Root cause: the perf python patcher NEVER touches `<video>`, but a prior define (toggle/restore) had left the virtio-gpu boot display on bus 0x06 — the GPU's reserved guest slot, read from the saved device fragment (`profiles/win11/devices/gpu.xml` guest address `bus='0x06'`). In mode=on the GPU hostdev is stripped, so bus 0x06 is FREE at define time and the boot display sitting there is accepted; the later live-attach hot-attach of the GPU at its fixed keep-guest-address 0x06 then collides -> libvirt rejects the attach -> hotplug "does not work". The earlier R44 pin (`_lg_set_vm_display_live_attach`) only avoided the GPU bus when the `VFIO_LA_RESERVED_GUEST_BUSES` env var was set, and only the install paths set it — the toggle path (`profile_apply_mode`), the perf define, and `remove_looking_glass`'s restore never did, so a boot display could land on 0x06 and stay there.
  - fix: new shared `_la_repair_video_off_gpu_bus()` helper is a single backstop that runs on EVERY define of a live-attach VM. It reads the reserved GPU+audio guest buses DIRECTLY from the profile fragments (not only the env var — so the toggle/restore paths that do NOT export `VFIO_LA_RESERVED_GUEST_BUSES` still work) UNION the env var UNION the buses of PCI hostdevs still in the XML (the GPU when cold-attached), then re-pins the `<video>` boot display off any reserved bus to the smallest-index free non-reserved `pcie-root-port` (a port's child bus = its controller index in hex). Exit codes: 0 = re-pinned (file written); 3 = already safe / no-op (not a live-attach VM with a fragment, video=none, video already off every reserved bus, or an address-less video with no free reserved bus to proactive-pin); 1 = the video sits ON a reserved bus but no free non-reserved root-port was found (caller warns / skips the define). Wired into 4 call sites: `profile_apply_mode` AFTER the device-diff safety invariant passes (so it cannot trip it) + its no-edit/already-in-target-mode branch as a define-only repair pass; the perf patcher after the python succeeds (before `virt-xml-validate`); and `remove_looking_glass`'s restore after `_lg_restore_vm_display` (which flips none->virtio with NO address, so libvirt would otherwise auto-place the restored boot display on the freed 0x06).
  - live fix: re-ran `sudo /usr/local/bin/vfio --live-attach-on` on the shut-off win11 (already mode=on, GPU stripped) — the no-edit path ran the new repair define-only pass, re-pinned the boot display off 0x06 -> bus 0x08 (a free non-reserved root-port), redefined the VM. Verified: video now at `bus='0x08'`, NO PCI address on bus 0x06 in win11, GPU hostdev still stripped (mode=on intact), win11 still shut off — the `double-use PCI address 0000:06:00.0` collision is resolved so the GPU hot-attach will land at its keep-guest-address without collision.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean on `vfio.sh` + all regression scripts; full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 — 22 steps PASS, 0 FAIL, shellcheck green, all 16 `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY. 20 new R48i assertions in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` (static: helper defined, documents the `double-use PCI address 0000:06:00.0` root cause, reads the GPU+audio fragment guest buses directly, re-pins an already-addressed video off the reserved bus, skips video=none, skips when no reserved buses, repair wired in >=5 places [def + 4 call sites: toggle after-safety, toggle no-edit, perf, LG restore], each of the 4 call-site wiring checks via extracted function bodies; functional: the repair helper re-pins an on-reserved-bus video off 0x06 to a free root-port + keeps the virtio model, is a no-op for video=none [exit 3, no address added], is a no-op with no fragment + no env [exit 3]; end-to-end `profile_apply_mode` toggle-on re-pins the boot display off 0x06 while the extra disk + LG shmem survive [repair runs after the safety invariant] and the GPU is stripped in mode=on — all with a fake virsh + fake virt-xml-validate + redirected profile dir, no root). Deployed to `/usr/local/bin/vfio` (live repair verified on win11: 0x06 -> 0x08).

- 2026-09-03 18:14 UTC: Closed the residual Windows `VM=yes` detection gap + fixed the Looking Glass summary detection + added a compile-client-first disclaimer in `vfio.sh` (R48g: MAC OUI spoof + SMBIOS type 2/3/17 + anchored LG detection + LG prerequisite banner):
  - stealth MAC OUI spoof: the stealth/perf python patcher (`install_stealth_vm_tuning`) changed the NIC *model* to `e1000e` but NEVER touched the MAC address, so the QEMU/KVM OUI prefix `52:54:00` (the single most reliable automated VM tell — a real desktop NIC never has it) remained and Windows detection tools still reported a VM. The patcher now replaces any QEMU/Hyper-V/VBox OUI prefix (`52:54:00` / `00:15:5d` / `08:00:27` / `00:50:56` / `00:0c:29` / `00:1c:42`) with a real-vendor OUI from a registered pool (Intel/ASUS/Realtek/Gigabyte) + deterministic last-3-octets derived from the VM UUID (sha256), so a re-tune does NOT churn the MAC (keeps DHCP leases / the Windows network profile stable). Any other (real/user-set) MAC is left untouched. Live-verified on win11: MAC `52:54:00:29:f7:ac` -> `00:1a:a0:8a:77:3e`, stable across a re-tune (the spoofed OUI is not a QEMU prefix so the guard skips it).
  - SMBIOS type 2/3/17 spoof: the patcher spoofed SMBIOS type 0 (BIOS) + type 1 (system) but NOT type 2 (baseboard) / type 3 (chassis) / type 17 (memory), so `Win32_BaseBoard.Manufacturer` / `Win32_SystemEnclosure.Manufacturer` / the physical memory arrays still reported QEMU defaults — another residual VM tell. The patcher now also emits QEMU `-smbios type=2` (baseboard: manufacturer + product + version + serial from the host DMI `board_vendor`/`board_name`/`board_version`), `type=3` (chassis: manufacturer + `type=3` = Desktop + version + serial), `type=17` (memory: `Kingston` + `speed=3600` + serial). The bash wrapper exports new `VFIO_STEALTH_DMI_BOARD_VENDOR`/`_BOARD_NAME`/`_BOARD_VERSION`/`_CHASSIS_VENDOR` env vars (read from `/sys/class/dmi/id/board_*` + `chassis_vendor`, with hardcoded ASUS TUF X570-PLUS fallbacks). All serials are derived from the VM UUID hash (sha256, stable across re-tunes — the old `secrets.token_hex` would change every run and churn the Windows HW fingerprint / digital license). Live-verified on win11: type 2/3/17 all present, validates via `virt-xml-validate`.
  - honest ceiling note: the XML-level fixes (MAC, SMBIOS 0/1/2/3/17, NIC model, CPUID hypervisor bit, vmport off) close the CASUAL VM detection (CPUID + SMBIOS + MAC + NIC model). Deeper tells (the SMBIOS VM bit, BIOS vendor `SeaBIOS`, ACPI OEM `BOCHS`, the FADT `QEMU` hypervisor-vendor string, the disk model `QEMU HARDDISK`, the `QEMU0001/0002/VGID` ACPI device _HIDs) require a PATCHED QEMU binary (e.g. `dsecuma/qemu-anti-detection`) — they cannot be defeated from libvirt XML alone. Timing attacks (RDTSC) cannot be fully hidden at all. The script's existing disclaimer already states this is cosmetic realism, not an anti-cheat bypass. (Also clarified for the user: "virtualization enabled" in Task Manager / `msinfo32` is the CPU SVM/VT-x bit, which every modern physical PC shows — it is NOT a VM tell; `host-passthrough` deliberately passes it through so the VM looks consistent with bare metal.)
  - anchored Looking Glass detection: `_vm_checklist_records` + `looking_glass_status` matched ANY `ivshmem-plain` device and grabbed the size from ANY `<size>` element in the whole XML, so a VM with a DIFFERENT ivshmem device false-positived as Looking Glass and the reported size could be wrong. New shared `_lg_vm_shmem_info()` helper anchors detection to the `<shmem name='looking-glass'>` SPECIFICALLY and pulls the size from THAT element (handles both libvirt single-quote post-define + ElementTree double-quote pre-define styles); the checklist + `looking_glass_status` now consume it. Also anchored `_lg_vm_has_shmem()` (used in the remove dedup loop) to the looking-glass name so a non-LG ivshmem no longer spins the detach loop, and removed the dead `_lg_vm_shmem_size()` helper (no callers).
  - compile-client-first disclaimer: `install_looking_glass` now shows a prominent `PREREQUISITE` banner explaining the VM-side `<shmem>` only exposes the framebuffer — it is the `looking-glass-client` BINARY that reads it and renders the window; without the client you see a black window / "cannot find shared memory" and nothing. It checks if the client is already compiled and points to `--install-looking-glass-client` / menu option 18 if not. (Made SMART in R48h — see the next entry.)
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean; the extracted stealth python heredoc `py_compile` clean; full 16-suite `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 — all `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY, shellcheck green; Looking Glass smoke PASS. 27 new R48g assertions (22 in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` — static: MAC spoof `_QEMU_OUIS` + `mac_el.set('address',_new_mac)`, SMBIOS type 2/3/17 emit, UUID-hash stable serials, board/chassis DMI env exports, `_lg_vm_shmem_info` helper defined + anchored to `looking-glass`, checklist consumes it, `looking_glass_status` consumes it, disclaimer text + install flag; functional: extracted patcher compiles, replaces the QEMU MAC OUI with a real-vendor OUI, adds SMBIOS type 2/3/17, is idempotent (MAC stable across re-tunes), `_lg_vm_shmem_info` reports the looking-glass size (64) not the other ivshmem (128), rejects a non-looking-glass ivshmem as NOT configured; 5 in `regression/looking-glass-regression.sh` — helper defined + anchored + checklist consumes it + disclaimer + install flag). Deployed to `/usr/local/bin/vfio` (all markers verified). Live-verified against win11: MAC spoofed, SMBIOS 0/1/2/3/17 present, LG shmem 64M, hypervisor-hide core intact.

- 2026-09-03 10:34 UTC: Added a context-aware Resizable BAR (ReBAR) disclaimer to the interactive `--menu` GUI in `vfio.sh` (R48b: tell the operator about the ReBAR / AMD black-screen caveat before they pick an action):
  - new `_menu_rebar_disclaimer()` helper reads `GUEST_GPU_VENDOR_ID` from the conf (awk, no sourcing) + the world-readable `/proc/cmdline` (no root), mirroring the R45 ReBAR check already used by `live_attach_status` so the menu + the tray applet agree. It is vendor-aware: AMD (1002) + `amdgpu.rebar=0` MISSING -> WARN that amdgpu auto-resizes BAR0 to full VRAM on load, which breaks the Windows AMD display driver (the display goes black even though the driver loads), and points at `sudo vfio --install-dynamic-binding --amd-rebar` as the fix; AMD + the param present -> OK (BAR0 stays at the BIOS size); NVIDIA (10de) / Intel (8086) -> OK with an explicit note that those vendors handle a resized BAR cleanly so the AMD black-screen issue does NOT apply there; no conf / unknown vendor -> silent.
  - two render modes: `full` (default) emits `note()` lines for the menu header (the CLI/plain-text path), and `compact` emits one plain line with NO color (so whiptail does not truncate the label at an ESC byte) that is embedded in the `select_from_list` prompt via a real newline. `vfio_menu` calls BOTH: the full notes print in the header, AND the compact line is prepended to the "What do you want to do?" prompt so the disclaimer is visible INSIDE the whiptail dialog (the header notes only render on the terminal behind the dialog), not just on the CLI. The header refreshes every loop iteration so the disclaimer tracks state changes (e.g. after `--amd-rebar` adds the param, it flips to OK).
