- 2026-08-31 11:55 UTC: Pinned the live-attach virtio-gpu boot display OFF the GPU/audio reserved guest PCI bus in `vfio.sh` (hotplug "no driver installed" / Code 28 root cause on a primed VM):
  - bug (hot-plugged GPU landed on the wrong guest bus): when live-attach mode=on strips the GPU hostdev from the persistent XML, the GPU's dedicated `pcie-root-port` (e.g. guest bus 0x06) is FREE at boot. `_lg_set_vm_display_live_attach` adds a virtio-gpu boot display (so Windows is not headless and the hot-attached GPU can initialize) but did NOT pin the `<video>` device to a specific PCI address, so libvirt auto-placed it on the now-free GPU root-port (bus 0x06). The live-attach helper then hot-attached the GPU wanting its fixed guest address bus 0x06 (`VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_KEEP_GUEST_ADDR=1`, the earlier keep-guest-address fix) -> COLLISION -> libvirt reassigned the hot-plugged GPU to a different guest bus (observed bus 0x07) -> Windows saw a NEW device instance path instead of the one it installed the AMD driver against (the cold-attach slot bus 0x06) -> `amdkmdag` did NOT start -> Device Manager "No drivers are installed for this device" (Code 28), black screen. The keep-guest-address fix was correct in intent but was DEFEATED by the unpinned boot display stealing the GPU's slot.
  - fix: `_lg_set_vm_display_live_attach` now pins the virtio-gpu `<video><address>` to a free `pcie-root-port` whose child bus is NOT the GPU's and NOT the audio's reserved guest bus. Reserved buses come from a new `VFIO_LA_RESERVED_GUEST_BUSES` env var (comma-sep "0xNN") UNION the buses of any PCI hostdevs still in the XML. A pcie-root-port's child bus = its controller index in hex (standard libvirt q35 allocation). The smallest-index free, non-reserved root-port is chosen; the video address is set/updated to it (idempotent — no change if already pinned to the same bus). If no suitable port is found the video is left unpinned (legacy behavior); a wrong pin would fail `virt-xml-validate` and the caller already falls back to the unpatched XML, so this is safe. With the boot display pinned elsewhere, bus 0x06 stays free and the hot-attached GPU lands at its keep-guest-address slot -> Windows recognizes the device -> the AMD driver starts.
  - plumbing: new `_la_fragment_guest_bus()` helper reads the GUEST PCI bus (the direct-child `<address type='pci' bus='0xNN'>`, NOT the `<source>` host BDF) from a device fragment. `install_live_attach` and `install_looking_glass` read the GPU + audio guest buses from the per-domain profile fragments and export `VFIO_LA_RESERVED_GUEST_BUSES` around the `_lg_set_vm_display_live_attach` call (restored/unset after). `_lg_set_vm_display_live_attach` is the live-attach-only path (cold-attach uses `_lg_set_vm_display_none` = video=none), so cold-attach is unaffected.
  - note (this is the topology fix, not the driver): a hot-plugged GPU has no UEFI GOP framebuffer (OVMF only runs the option ROM for boot-present devices), so on a FRESH Windows install with no AMD driver the hot-plugged card is Code 28 regardless of slot (kubevirt #8733 confirms: passthrough display only works before the guest loads the vendor driver when OVMF runs the ROM at boot). This pin fixes the SLOT collision so that ONCE the AMD driver is bound to the cold-attach slot (one cold-attach boot + Adrenalin install), hotplug reuses that exact slot and the driver starts. Cold-attach (`--install-dynamic-binding`) remains the reliable path; hotplug needs that one priming boot.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity, no `-S`) clean on `vfio.sh` + all regression scripts. Added Run 5 to `regression/looking-glass-regression.sh`: a mock XML with `pcie-root-port` controllers 5/6/7 + GPU guest address bus 0x06 + audio bus 0x07, `VFIO_LA_RESERVED_GUEST_BUSES=0x06,0x07` -> the patcher exits 0 and the virtio-gpu is pinned to bus 0x05 (off both reserved buses). Smoke-tested `_la_fragment_guest_bus` in isolation (returns guest bus 0x06, not the source host bus 0x0e; empty for a missing file). Fixed two SC2155 (split `export VAR="$(...)"` into assign + export) and a pre-existing SC2154 in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` (the fix-#9 `virsh destroy` assertion used double quotes around `$_dom`, which expanded to empty and searched for `destroy ""` instead of the literal `destroy "$_dom"` — single-quoted the pattern, which also fixes the assertion correctness). Full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 — all `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY, shellcheck green.
- 2026-08-30 14:20 UTC: Replaced the R41/R42 frozen-full-domain-XML live-attach mode switch with a recognition + device-fragment model in `vfio.sh` (R44: live-attach mode switch by recognition + device fragments):
  - goal: the user asked that a live-attach toggle NEVER wipe unrelated VM edits. The R41/R42 switch `virsh define`d one of two frozen full-domain dumps (with-GPU / without-GPU) saved at install time, so any edit made afterward in virt-manager (RAM/CPU/disk, Looking Glass `<shmem>`, stealth/perf tuning) was lost the first time the tray icon or `--live-attach-toggle` flipped the mode. R44 makes the CURRENT libvirt domain XML (`virsh dumpxml`) the single source of truth and the only on-disk state is small device fragments (`profiles/<dom>/devices/{gpu,gpu-audio}.xml`) + a derived manifest — a mode switch edits the live XML by inserting/removing ONLY the GPU (+ audio) hostdevs, so every unrelated device survives.
  - recognition + apply: new `profile_recognize` reads the live XML and classifies the mode (has GPU hostdev → `off`; no GPU but a saved fragment → `on`; neither → `unknown`/refuse), writes/updates a per-domain manifest, and reconciles the mode file (live XML wins; prints a MISMATCH and rewrites the file when they disagree). new `profile_apply_mode` does the in-place edit: `on` extracts the GPU (+ first audio) fragment from the CURRENT XML (overwriting so rom file=/address tweaks stay current) then strips ONLY those hostdevs; `off` re-inserts them from the saved fragment (one-release fallback: if the fragment is missing it tries once to recover it from an old full backup, then refuses rather than invent a hostdev from vendor:device IDs). Supporting helpers `_la_profile_dir` / `_la_manifest_read`/`_write` / `_la_extract_hostdev_fragment` / `_la_audio_bdfs` / `_la_devices_signature` (reusing R43b's `_xml_has_gpu_hostdev` as the GPU-detection primitive). `live_attach_toggle` now recognizes each shut-off VM, resolves the target (on/off force, or toggle = flip the RECOGNIZED mode), applies it, and flips the global mode file ONLY if ≥1 shut-off VM was actually redefined (a running/refused/unknown run does NOT silently flip the file — it prints a mismatch instead). `live_attach_status` reports `recognized=` alongside `mode_file=`/`xml_has_gpu`/`gpu_fragment`/`gpu` liveness, and the JSON mirrors it.
  - safety invariants: `profile_apply_mode` runs shut-off only, computes a device-diff signature of everything EXCEPT the GPU/audio hostdevs before vs after the edit (whitespace-normalized so `virsh dumpxml` vs Python `ET.write` formatting differences don't false-trip), REFUSES to define if anything else changed, and `virt-xml-validate`s before `virsh define`. The live-attach helper heredoc now resolves the per-domain `profiles/<DOMAIN>/devices/gpu.xml` at runtime (flat-path `LIVE_ATTACH_GPU_XML` symlink fallback) and strips the guest PCI address before `attach-device --live` (the saved fragment keeps the original slot for the define path; the live hot-attach must not pin it). The libvirt hook gained a recognition guard: if `mode=on` but the XML already has the GPU, it does NOT launch a second hotplug. `install_live_attach` no longer writes the with/without-GPU full-domain dumps (it extracts fragments + creates the manifest + symlinks the flat paths); `remove_live_attach` applies mode=off FIRST (via the fragment, never by `virsh define`-ing an old full backup), then deletes the profile dirs + artifacts. R42's `_la_ensure_with_gpu_variant` is removed (superseded by `profile_apply_mode`). The PySide6 tray applet shows the RECOGNIZED mode and turns yellow ("XML not updated yet") when the mode file disagrees with the recognized mode instead of green.
  - bugs found + fixed during integration: (1) `_la_devices_signature` was fed the temp-file PATH string (`printf '%s' "$_tmp"`) instead of its CONTENT for the after-signature, so `sig_after` was always empty → the safety invariant tripped on every toggle; fixed to read the file (`<"$_tmp"`). (2) the invariant comparison itself read `"_sig_after"` (a LITERAL string, missing `$`) so it compared the real before-signature against the text `_sig_after` and ALWAYS refused even after (1) was fixed; fixed to `"$_sig_after"`. (3) `_la_write_mode`'s conf-key flip called `rewrite_conf_key` unguarded, and `rewrite_conf_key`'s `cat >"$CONF_FILE"` fails when the conf is not writable (a non-root regression run, where sourcing vfio.sh resets `CONF_FILE` to the real `/etc` path) → under `set -euo pipefail` this aborted `profile_recognize`, violating its own documented "never aborts the caller" contract; guarded the call (`|| true`) so a non-writable conf can never abort the best-effort mode write. (4) the one-release fallback hardcoded `/var/lib/vfio-dynamic/live-attach-{backup,vm-with-gpu}-<dom>.xml` with no env override, so a non-root test whose mock GPU BDF coincided with a real system backup (0000:0e:00.0 here) silently satisfied the fallback and re-inserted the GPU instead of refusing; added a `VFIO_LA_LEGACY_DIR` override (mirrors the existing `VFIO_LA_PROFILES_DIR` / `LIVE_ATTACH_VM_LIST` pattern) so the test can redirect the lookup to an empty dir. (5) a pre-existing shellcheck SC2157 in `_la_extract_hostdev_fragment` (`[[ -n "_needle" ]]` literal-string guard that never checked the args) fixed to `"$_needle"`/`"$_out"`.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity, no `-S` — the binder scans vfio.sh + all regression scripts) clean. Rewrote the `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` functional block onto the new model (dropped the R41/R42 full-dump assertions; added recognition/fragment/safety-invariant/missing-fragment-refuse/fallback/hook-guard/status/tray-mismatch assertions + a functional acceptance test: toggle off→on→off→on preserves an added `<disk>` + Looking-Glass `<shmem>` while only the GPU hostdev is added/removed, using a fake virsh + fake virt-xml-validate + redirected profile/legacy dirs — no root, no real libvirt; the assertions use a quote-agnostic `_hasq` helper because Python `ET.write` round-trips single-quoted attributes to double quotes). Updated `regression/vm-stage-backup-regression.sh` (R44 live-attach fallback) and `regression/live-attach-tray-smoke.sh` (R44 status shape + recognized mode). Full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 — all `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY, shellcheck green. - 2026-08-30 10:58 UTC: Fixed the live-attach tray toggle leaving the GPU removed from virt-manager when turning hotplug OFF in `vfio.sh` (R43b: split-attribute BDF detection in `_la_ensure_with_gpu_variant`):
  - bug (toggle OFF did not restore the GPU): when the operator clicked the system-tray icon (or `--live-attach-toggle`) to turn hotplug OFF, the GPU stayed removed from virt-manager instead of being restored to the VM XML. The user reported: "when i press trayicon live attach is still removing on off pcie gpu from virtmanager i want it to keep and still keep off hotplugg while it is off."
  - root cause: `_la_ensure_with_gpu_variant()` (the R42 helper that guarantees toggle OFF always restores the GPU) checked whether a saved XML variant "has the GPU" with `grep -Fixq "$_gpu_bdf" "$_with_gpu"` — a LITERAL grep for the BDF string (e.g. `0000:0e:00.0`). But libvirt splits the PCI address across `domain='0x0000' bus='0x0e' slot='0x00' function='0x0'` attributes, so the combined BDF string is NEVER present in a real `virsh dumpxml`. Every other GPU-detection site in the script (`_vm_is_guest_gpu_vm`, `install_live_attach`, `install_hypervisor_hiding`, `install_virtio_win_guest_agent`) reconstructs the BDF with an awk parser first, then greps the reconstructed list — this one function grepped the raw XML directly, so its fast path AND rebuild sources 1/2 ALWAYS failed. Toggle OFF then fell through to source 3 (re-inject the GPU from the saved standalone device XMLs), and if `LIVE_ATTACH_GPU_XML` was missing/empty it returned 1 → `live_attach_toggle` skipped the VM with a WARN → the VM stayed in its mode=on (GPU-stripped) state while the mode file was flipped to `off` anyway. Net effect: hotplug said "off" but the GPU was still removed from virt-manager.
  - fix: new shared `_xml_has_gpu_hostdev()` helper reads VM XML on stdin, reconstructs every PCI hostdev source BDF with the SAME proven awk parser, and returns 0 only if one matches `$1`. All three GPU-presence checks in `_la_ensure_with_gpu_variant` (the fast path, source 1 = legacy backup, source 2 = current dumpxml) now route through this helper instead of the broken literal grep. With the fast path working, an existing valid with-GPU variant is recognized and `virsh define`d directly — toggle OFF restores the GPU to virt-manager even when the saved device XMLs are missing.
  - design note: this was a pre-existing R42 bug (the literal grep was always wrong), not a regression from R43's once-only backup change — R43 only changed the install-time *write* path, not the toggle-time *read* path. R43b surfaced because the user toggled for the first time after the R43 work; the fix is independent and also benefits pre-R43 installs.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity) clean on `vfio.sh`. Extended `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` with an R43b block (static: helper defined, ensure helper routes all 3 GPU checks through the helper, the old literal `grep -Fixq "$_gpu_bdf"` is gone; functional: on a mock libvirt XML with split-attribute PCI address the helper detects `0000:0e:00.0` while a literal grep does NOT — proving the bug premise — plus negative cases for an absent BDF, a hostdev-less XML, and an empty needle) — rc=0, all R43b PASS, ends with the passed banner. `live-attach` / `dynamic-binding` / `ultimate-perf` / `vm-stage-backup` regressions all still PASS. README: added an R43b note to the system-tray toggle section (additive, existing text intact).
- 2026-08-30 09:18 UTC: Unified the VM-XML stage backups to ONE pristine per stage + a single `--reset` fresh-start wipe in `vfio.sh` (R43: shared once-only writer + unified reset wipe):
  - goal: the user asked that each stage (stealth / perf / live-attach) store exactly ONE backup per VM (no per-run accumulation), that the backup be taken at every trigger point (the standalone `--install-*` flags AND the `--menu` GUI AND the dynamic install flow), that a re-run NOT back up again, and that `--reset` delete ALL xml backups for a fresh start (a re-install rebuilds them from the live VM XML). Stealth/perf already enforced once-only via R37's `_vm_is_*_tuned` guard + fixed-name `${dom}_stealth.xml` / `${dom}_perf.xml`; live-attach already used one fixed `live-attach-backup-<dom>.xml`. The gaps were consistency + a single rule: live-attach OVERWROTE its with-GPU backup every run (no once-only guard), and the `--reset` wipe was split across two helpers.
  - once-only writer: new shared `_save_pristine_vm_backup()` helper (write-if-absent, atomic via `write_file_atomic`, dry-run safe) — writes the pristine pre-stage VM XML ONLY when no backup yet exists, so each VM keeps exactly one pristine snapshot per stage, taken the first time that stage touches it, never re-backed-up. `install_live_attach` now routes BOTH its pristine with-GPU snapshots (the legacy `live-attach-backup-<dom>.xml` and the R41 `live-attach-vm-with-gpu-<dom>.xml` mode=off variant) through this helper instead of bare `write_file_atomic` calls, so live-attach matches the stealth/perf once-only rule. The without-GPU variant (the stripped mode=on state, not a pristine backup) is still regenerated each run the VM is (re)processed. Stealth/perf keep their richer `_vm_is_*_tuned` guard (which also blocks fabricating a "pristine" backup from already-tuned XML in the orphaned case).
  - trigger points: confirmed the menu GUI (`vfio_menu` options 4/6/8) and the dynamic install flow (`install_dynamic_binding_from_existing_config`) route through the SAME `install_stealth_vm_tuning` / `install_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning` / `install_live_attach` functions that snapshot the pristine XML — so backups are already taken at every trigger point, not only the standalone flags. No new call sites needed; the once-only helper makes the behavior uniform.
  - unified reset wipe: replaced `_remove_vm_tuning_backups` (R37, stealth/perf only) with `_wipe_vm_stage_backups()` — a single fresh-start sweep of EVERY stage backup XML: stealth (`${dom}_stealth.xml` + legacy `~/Desktop/*_stealth_*.xml`), perf (`${dom}_perf.xml` + legacy `~/Desktop/*_perf_*.xml`), and live-attach (`live-attach-backup-*.xml` + `live-attach-vm-with-gpu-*.xml` + `live-attach-vm-without-gpu-*.xml`). The live-attach dir is derived from `dirname "$LIVE_ATTACH_GPU_XML"` (not hardcoded) so a regression test can redirect it to a temp dir without root. It SPARES the hugepages owned-count files (`*_perf_hugepages_owned.txt` — NOT backups; hugepage accounting freed separately by `_reset_perf_hugepages_all`, called right after). `reset_vfio_all` now calls `_wipe_vm_stage_backups` (live-attach artifacts are also removed by `remove_live_attach` above it; this is the defense-in-depth fresh-start sweep for every stage). The reset NOTE block was updated to name all three stages.
  - design note (why not physically merge the dirs): the stealth/perf backup dirs are conf-configurable (`STEALTH_VM_BACKUP_DIR` / `ULTIMATE_PERF_VM_BACKUP_DIR`) and the perf dir also holds the hugepages owned-count files entangled with the reboot-persistent boot-reserve registry + the generated boot oneshot. Physically merging them into one fixed dir would retire that configurability and risk the hugepages boot-reserve registry, so R43 keeps the existing dirs and achieves the "one place to wipe" semantically via the single `_wipe_vm_stage_backups` helper instead. A physical merge into one `VFIO_VM_BACKUP_ROOT` can be a separate, carefully-tested change if the per-layer dir config is ever retired.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity, no `-S`) clean on `vfio.sh` and the new regression script. New `regression/vm-stage-backup-regression.sh` (static wiring + functional: `_save_pristine_vm_backup` first-write / once-only-keep / empty-path no-op / dry-run skip / real-write-after-dry-run; `_wipe_vm_stage_backups` removes every stage .xml across redirected stealth/perf/legacy/live-attach temp dirs while SPARING the hugepages owned .txt, and is a no-op on an empty slate) — rc=0, all PASS, ends with the passed banner (FAIL SUMMARY guard in place). Updated 5 stale assertions in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` (the R23 `write_file_atomic "$_backup_xml"` atomic-write check → R43 once-only-helper routing; the R37 `_remove_vm_tuning_backups` defined/called/legacy-glob checks → R43 `_wipe_vm_stage_backups`) — `live-attach-regression.sh`, `dynamic-binding-regression.sh`, `ultimate-perf-regression.sh` all still PASS. README: new "What's new" paragraph + a "VM XML backups — one pristine per stage + reset fresh-start (R43)" note in the Stealth/perf VM tuning section (additive, existing text intact).
- 2026-08-29 19:08 UTC: Hardened the R41 live-attach tray toggle in `vfio.sh` (R41 follow-up: atomic mode-file write, polkit action-id DRY, tray single-instance guard, `--live-attach-status --json`, README menu reconciliation):
  - atomic mode-file write: `_la_write_mode` now writes `/var/lib/vfio-dynamic/live-attach-mode` via the shared `write_file_atomic` helper (temp-file-then-install, mode 0644 root:root = world-readable) instead of a bare `printf >file` + `chmod`, so a crash mid-toggle can't leave a partial/empty mode file the default-on fail-safe would then mis-read as "on".
  - polkit action-id DRY: the `/usr/share/polkit-1/actions/dev.vfio.live-attach-toggle.policy` heredoc is now unquoted (`<<POLEOF`) and emits `<action id="$LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT_ACTION">` so the action id is single-sourced from the constant (the rest of the XML has no $/backtick, so expansion is safe). Previously the id was hardcoded in the heredoc while `LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT_ACTION` held the same string.
  - tray single-instance guard: the PySide6 tray applet gained an `ensure_single_instance()` Qt local-socket guard (`QLocalServer`/`QLocalSocket` on `/tmp/vfio-hotplug-tray.sock`) so a second launch (autostart + manual, or two autostart hits) connects to the running instance's socket and exits quietly instead of showing a second icon. `QLocalServer.removeServer` cleans any stale socket from a crashed previous instance; the server is parented to the app so it lives for the whole session. Best-effort: if `QtNetwork` is unavailable the guard is skipped (the tray still runs). Verified `PySide6.QtNetwork` imports on this host.
  - `--live-attach-status --json`: `live_attach_status` now emits a JSON object `{"installed":0|1,"mode":"on|off|unknown","vms":[{"domain":"<dom>","state":"<state>"},...]}` when `--json` is set (values `json_escape`d), so other tooling/scripts — not just the tray's zenity dialog — can consume the state. `parse_args` now allows `--json` with `--live-attach-status` (the validation message + the `--json`/`--live-attach-status` help blocks updated); `--json` with any other unsupported mode is still rejected. KEY=value output is unchanged when `--json` is absent.
  - README menu reconciliation: the "Menu options" list was stale (1–13, Exit=13) vs the real `vfio_menu` (21 entries); updated to the full 21 (added Full configure recommended-defaults, Apply/Revert ultimate-perf VM tuning, Set up/Remove Looking Glass, Install/Remove looking-glass-client, Toggle live-attach hotplug; Exit=21), and added the `--live-attach-status` line to the `--json` common-flags bullet. Additive — existing descriptions kept, just renumbered + gaps filled.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity, no `-S` — the binder scans vfio.sh + all regression scripts) clean; tray `py_compile` clean (incl. the new single-instance guard). Extended `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` with 10 R41+ assertions (atomic `write_file_atomic`, unquoted `<<POLEOF`, `<action id="$LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT_ACTION">` + no hardcoded action id, `--json` allowed for live-attach-status, `live_attach_status` JSON branch + `"vms": [`, tray `ensure_single_instance`/`QLocalServer`/`SOCK_NAME`) — rc=0, 297 PASS. Extended `regression/live-attach-tray-smoke.sh` (single-instance greps + `--live-attach-status --json` shape) — SMOKE SUMMARY: PASS. Updated the stale `--json` validation assertion in `regression/custom-kernel-params-regression.sh` to the new message. Full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 (0 FAIL SUMMARY, 1846 PASS).
- 2026-08-29 18:42 UTC: Added a system-tray on/off toggle for the live-attach hotplug in `vfio.sh` (R41: real mode switch + PySide6 tray applet), replacing the backup-restore toggle with a persisted on/off mode the user can flip from the desktop:
  - mode switch (not backup-restore): `install_live_attach` now saves BOTH per-VM XML variants up front — `live-attach-vm-with-gpu-<dom>.xml` (original dumpxml, GPU present → mode=off, normal dynamic binding) and `live-attach-vm-without-gpu-<dom>.xml` (GPU-stripped → mode=on, VM boots on a virtual display and the helper hot-attaches the GPU once Windows is up). `live_attach_toggle` just `virsh define`s the right saved variant and flips the mode — a positive, symmetric switch instead of the backup-restore dance. The legacy `live-attach-backup-<dom>.xml` is kept as a READ fallback for `remove_live_attach` on pre-R41 setups. The mode lives in a world-readable file `/var/lib/vfio-dynamic/live-attach-mode` (single line `on`/`off`, 0644) so the user-session tray applet and the libvirt hook can read it WITHOUT root and WITHOUT sourcing the root-owned conf, plus a `VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_MODE` conf key (flipped by `_la_write_mode` via `rewrite_conf_key`). New constants `LIVE_ATTACH_MODE_FILE` / `LIVE_ATTACH_TRAY` / `LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT` / `LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT_ACTION` + helpers `_la_read_mode` / `_la_write_mode`. Default-on fail-safe: a missing or garbage mode file is treated as `on` (pre-R41 = hotplug active), so the hook and `live_attach_status` never mis-report a pre-R41 setup as off.
  - hook gating: the libvirt qemu hook `prepare` branch now reads the mode file and launches the live-attach helper ONLY when `mode=on` AND `VFIO_DYNAMIC_LIVE_ATTACH=1` AND the domain is in the live-attach VM list; otherwise it falls through to standard dynamic binding (`vm_uses_guest_gpu` → `--bind-now`). Pre-R41 behavior (helper always launched when live-attach active) is preserved because the default mode is `on`.
  - CLI flags: `--live-attach-on` / `--live-attach-off` (set the mode explicitly, idempotent), `--live-attach-toggle` (flip the current mode — also what the tray calls), `--live-attach-status` (machine-readable: `installed=0|1`, `mode=on|off`, one `vm=<dom>:<state>` line per guest-GPU VM; read-only, no root). Wired into `parse_args`, the MODE comment, main dispatch, `vfio_menu` (new entry "Toggle live-attach hotplug on/off", Exit renumbered), fish/bash/zsh completions, and the usage one-liner + help blocks. `--live-attach-status` added to the DRY_RUN + modprobe-skip lists. `verify_setup` / `--detect` now print `INFO: Live-attach mode: <on|off>`.
  - PySide6 system-tray applet: `install_live_attach_tray()` generates `/usr/local/bin/vfio-hotplug-tray` — a PySide6 `QSystemTrayIcon` app (KDE Plasma 6 uses the StatusNotifierItem protocol natively via Qt6, so the icon actually appears; XEmbed / `zenity --notification` would be invisible on Plasma 6). It reads the world-readable mode file (no root), refreshes icon/tooltip every 2s, and the "Toggle hotplug" menu item runs a `zenity --question` confirmation → `pkexec vfio --live-attach-toggle` → `notify-send` result (normal urgency on success, critical on failure); a "Status…" item shows the full `--live-attach-status` output in a zenity info dialog. A per-user autostart desktop file (`~/.config/autostart/vfio-hotplug-tray.desktop`, installed for `SUDO_USER`) starts it on next Plasma login, and the installer best-effort launches one instance now (Wayland env-aware via `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`). `remove_live_attach_tray()` removes the tray + polkit + autostart (all `/home/*` users, best-effort) and pkill's running instances; wired into `install_live_attach` + `remove_live_attach` + `reset_vfio_all` (the `--reset` rm list now includes `$LIVE_ATTACH_MODE_FILE` / `$LIVE_ATTACH_TRAY` / `$LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT`).
  - polkit policy: a scoped `/usr/share/polkit-1/actions/dev.vfio.live-attach-toggle.policy` (action `dev.vfio.live-attach-toggle`) allows `pkexec` to run ONLY `/usr/local/bin/vfio --live-attach-toggle` with `auth_admin_keep` (so repeated tray toggles don't re-prompt every time) + a friendly KDE auth message + `allow_gui=true`; without it pkexec falls back to generic `org.freedesktop.policykit.exec` auth (still works, just re-prompts).
  - best-effort / non-fatal: if PySide6 (`python3-pyside6`), `zenity`, `pkexec`/polkit, or the self-installed `vfio` CLI (`--install-self`) are absent, `install_live_attach_tray` notes it (with the distro install hint, e.g. `sudo dnf install python3-pyside6`) and returns 0 — the CLI toggle still works.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` (default severity, no `-S` — the binder scans vfio.sh + all regression scripts) clean on `vfio.sh` and all regression scripts; embedded tray python `py_compile` clean + PySide6 imports on this host. Extended `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` with a full R41 block (4 constants, mode helpers, toggle picks without/with-gpu variant + flips mode file, both named XML variants saved, hook reads mode + gates helper on mode=on, remove falls back to named with-gpu + removes variants glob + mode file + tray + flips VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_MODE off, reset rm includes the 3 artifacts, tray PySide6/zenity/pkexec/notify-send/MODE_FILE wiring, polkit auth_admin_keep + scoped exec.path/argv1, autostart, menu toggle entry + dispatch) — rc=0, 287 PASS. New `regression/live-attach-tray-smoke.sh` (extract the generated tray applet → py_compile → QSystemTrayIcon/MODE_FILE/pkexec/zenity/notify-send/VFIO_BIN greps → PySide6 import → `--live-attach-status` shape with no-conf/pre-R41-conf/mode-file-off) — SMOKE SUMMARY: PASS. Fixed two stale R27 exact-substring assertions in `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` (the MODE comment + bash-completion opts now include the Looking Glass entries R40b inserted, which broke the contiguous substring) and the matching stale substrings in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh`. Fixed an SC2034 (the `LIVE_ATTACH_POLKIT_ACTION` constant is now used in the polkit install message) and 6× SC2015 in the new tray smoke (grep&&ok||bad → if-then-else) so the binder's shellcheck step (no `-S`) stays green. Full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 (bash -n + shellcheck + all `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY, 1836 PASS). README: new `### System-tray hotplug on/off toggle (R41)` subsection + Quick links + TOC + "What's new" teaser + the 4 new flags in the usage one-liner (additive, existing text intact).
- 2026-08-29 13:42 UTC: Fixed the Looking Glass `video=none` + spice-local XML patcher so the patched VM XML actually validates, in `vfio.sh` (R40b: `_lg_set_vm_display_none` RNG-invalid leftover attributes):
  - bug (validation failure): `_lg_set_vm_display_none` flipped the `<video><model>` and spice `<listen>` `type` to `none` but left the active-variant attributes in place — `heads`/`primary` (and `vram`/`ram`/`vgamem`) on the video model and `address`/`port` on the spice listen. The libvirt Relax-NG schema forbids the `none` variants from carrying those attributes, so the element failed its schema branch and `virt-xml-validate` rejected the whole VM with `Extra element devices in interleave` / `Element domain failed to validate content` (the same symptom previously mis-triaged as a duplicate `<devices>` tag; the duplicate-devices bug was already fixed earlier — the patcher uses `root.find('devices')` and appends to the existing element — but the leftover-attribute root cause remained). Isolated empirically: stripping the extra attributes makes each `none` element validate on its own.
  - fix: new `_to_none(el)` helper inside the patcher normalizes an element to `type='none'` keeping ONLY the `type` attribute (deletes every other attribute), used for both the video model and the spice listen. Idempotency preserved — `changed` stays False (exit 3) only when the element is already `none` with no leftover attributes; a `none` element that still carries stale attrs (e.g. from a pre-fix run, or a manual edit) is now cleaned and reported as changed (exit 0). `_lg_restore_vm_display` is unchanged (it restores `type='virtio' heads='1' primary='yes'`, which the virtio branch allows) and already validated.
  - secondary fix (status accuracy): `looking_glass_status()` never reported `spice=local` because its detection was a broken `grep -qA1 "type='spice'" ... | grep -q "listen type='none'"` pipe — `-q` suppresses output so `-A1` emitted nothing and the second grep saw empty input. Replaced with two independent `grep -q` checks (spice graphics present AND a `listen type='none'`) so `spice=local` now shows in `--detect`/`--verify`.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on `vfio.sh`; embedded python `py_compile` clean; `regression/looking-glass-regression.sh` rc=0 (90 PASS, including `display-none XML validates (virt-xml-validate)` and the idempotent exit-3 / restore round-trip assertions); full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 (bash -n + shellcheck + all 16 `*-regression.sh` PASS, 0 FAIL SUMMARY); new `regression/looking-glass-smoke.sh` SMOKE SUMMARY: PASS (18 checks, locks in the bug guard: `none` model carries no leftover `heads`/`primary`, `none` listen carries no leftover `address`, patched + round-trip XML validates, idempotent). `live-attach-regression.sh` exact bash-opts/MODE-comment/usage substrings already include `--install-looking-glass-client`/`--remove-looking-glass-client` (verified PASS).
- 2026-08-28 18:41 UTC: Stopped the stealth/perf VM-tuning backups from littering `~/Desktop` and made the backup revert-safe in `vfio.sh` (R37: predefined backup dir + keep one pristine per VM):
  - bug (litter + broken revert): the stealth tuner (`install_stealth_vm_tuning`) and the ultimate-perf tuner (`install_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning`) wrote a NEW timestamped VM-XML backup to `$HOME/Desktop` on every run (`win11_stealth_<ts>.xml`, `win11_perf_<ts>.xml`), so the Desktop accumulated dozens of stale backups and never pruned. Worse: a re-tune wrote a backup of the ALREADY-TUNED XML, and `--reset-*-vm-tuning` reverted to the NEWEST backup by mtime — so it restored the tuned XML and revert was a silent no-op. The user called the custom XML-backup mechanism "dumb" and asked to use libvirt's predefined backup instead.
  - research (why NOT libvirt snapshots): investigated `virsh snapshot-create-as` / `virsh snapshot-revert` as the "libvirt-native backup". `snapshot-revert` is DESTRUCTIVE and reverts the DISK image (Red Hat docs: "any changes in the domain since the last snapshot was taken will be lost"; no `--config-only` flag). For a Windows VM with an in-progress AMD driver install, a pre-tuning snapshot would roll back the Windows disk on revert and WIPE the driver install — a real data-loss hazard for this exact VFIO setup (Red Hat also warns memory snapshots are problematic with VFIO passthrough devices). Conclusion: libvirt snapshots are the WRONG tool for config-only revert; the custom XML backup is the correct primitive (it reverts ONLY the VM config XML, never the disk). The real fix is to make it not dumb.
  - fix (4 improvements): (1) default backup dir → predefined `/var/lib/vfio-stealth-vm/backups` and `/var/lib/vfio-perf-vm/backups` instead of `$HOME/Desktop` (the `STEALTH_VM_BACKUP_DIR` / `ULTIMATE_PERF_VM_BACKUP_DIR` conf keys still override; `$HOME/Desktop` is kept only as a legacy READ fallback for backups/owned files created before this change); (2) keep ONE pristine backup per VM — fixed name `${_dom}_stealth.xml` / `${_dom}_perf.xml` (no timestamp), so the dir no longer accumulates; (3) state-aware: new `_vm_is_stealth_tuned` / `_vm_is_perf_tuned` boolean helpers (grep the same markers the status functions use) gate the backup write — only write when the VM is NOT already tuned (current dumpxml is pristine); on a re-tune KEEP the existing pristine backup (so revert still works); if already tuned with NO backup (orphaned), WARN and do NOT fabricate a "pristine" backup from already-tuned XML; (4) revert reads the fixed-name backup directly, falling back to the newest timestamped `*_stealth_*.xml` / `*_perf_*.xml` in the legacy `$HOME/Desktop` dir so EXISTING setups (pre-R37) can still revert. Fixes the latent re-tune-breaks-revert bug. Live-attach backup unchanged (already a fixed per-VM name in `/var/lib/vfio-dynamic`).
  - hugepages owned-file migration (safety-critical): the perf backup dir also holds `${_dom}_perf_hugepages_owned.txt`, which the reboot-persistent boot oneshot + the dynamic at-VM-start ensure sum across ALL dirs in `PERF_HP_DIRS_FILE`. The live setup had the owned file at `/root/Desktop/win11_perf_hugepages_owned.txt` (=16096) with the registry pointing at `/root/Desktop`; after the default-dir change a perf re-tune would write a NEW owned file to `/var/lib/vfio-perf-vm/backups` and register it, while the legacy owned file + registry entry remained → `sum_all_owned` double-counts = 32192 pages (64GB). New `_migrate_legacy_perf_owned <new_dir> <dom>` helper moves the legacy owned file to the resolved dir (or drops the stale copy if the new one already exists) and unregisters the legacy dir from `PERF_HP_DIRS_FILE` when it has no remaining owned files; idempotent. Called in the perf install loop (before reserving) and in `reset_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning` (before restoring) so the owned file is always in the resolved dir.
  - --reset now removes ALL stealth/perf VM-XML backups + --full also removes the CLI: `reset_vfio_all` now calls a new `_remove_vm_tuning_backups` helper that wipes the fixed-name pristine backups in the resolved dirs AND the legacy `~/Desktop` timestamped litter (`*_stealth*.xml` / `*_perf*.xml` / `*_stealth_*.xml` / `*_perf_*.xml`), for a clean slate. The hugepages owned files + boot-reserve service are LEFT in place (R35 design; use `--reset-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning` for those). `reset_vfio_all` gained an optional `full` arg: when set (via the new `--full` flag, or the menu "Reset everything" option), it ALSO removes the self-installed `vfio` CLI command (`/usr/local/bin/vfio`) + the fish/bash/zsh shell completions — a true "remove everything this script installed". The repo copy of `vfio.sh` is NEVER touched (only the installed command). The mid-wizard preflight reset (`preflight_existing_config_gate`) stays SCOPED (no `full` arg) so it does not nuke the CLI the user is actively configuring with. New `RESET_FULL` var + `--full` parse_args case + usage one-liner + help block + fish/bash/zsh completions. Without `--full`, `--reset` stays scoped to VFIO config + stealth/perf backups and hints at both `--reset --full` and `--uninstall-self`.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean on `vfio.sh`; full `regression/script.sh` binder rc=0 (all 13 regression scripts pass, including the in-flight R38 hugepages-strip assertions — R37 layered on top without conflict); all 3 smoke scripts PASS. Extended `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh` (R37 stealth block: `_vm_is_stealth_tuned` helper, fixed-name backup, no `$(date)`, pristine-keep, orphan warn, `/var/lib` default, fixed-name revert read + legacy fallback) and `regression/ultimate-perf-regression.sh` (R37 perf block: same + `_migrate_legacy_perf_owned` helper + install/revert migration calls + a functional migration test: moves owned file legacy→new, unregisters legacy dir, idempotent rc 0); added an `assert_contains_text` helper to `ultimate-perf-regression.sh` (it only had `assert_contains_file`). README update skipped per user.
- 2026-08-26 20:18 UTC: Fixed the live-attach (hotplug GPU) opt-out so selecting "no" (or `--no-live-attach`) actually reverts an already-active live-attach setup in `vfio.sh` (R36: live-attach opt-out reverts):
  - bug: in `install_dynamic_binding_from_existing_config`, the RX 9070 live-attach recommendation prompt's "no" branch only printed skip notes and NEVER reverted an already-active live-attach setup — so a user who had live-attach active and re-ran `--install-dynamic-binding` (or the menu "Switch to dynamic binding") and picked "no" was left with live-attach still on the system: the VM XML still had the GPU hostdev stripped (the VM kept booting on a virtual display), the live-attach helper + VM list + per-VM backup stayed on disk, and `VFIO_DYNAMIC_LIVE_ATTACH` stayed `1`. The user reported: "when i run dynamíc and select no for hotplug it still on the system does not switch." The sibling opt-ins (vBIOS `--no-vbios`, stealth `--no-stealth-vm-tuning`) both revert on "no"; live-attach was the inconsistent one.
  - fix: the live-attach block now (a) calls `remove_live_attach` on a prompt "no" — restoring the GPU hostdev to each VM's persistent XML from the per-VM `live-attach-backup-<dom>.xml` backup, detaching the virtio-win ISO CD-ROM, removing the helper/VM-list/device-XMLs/backups, and flipping `VFIO_DYNAMIC_LIVE_ATTACH=0`, so the VM switches back to normal dynamic binding; (b) honors two new override flags `--live-attach` (force the workflow on, regardless of GPU family) and `--no-live-attach` (force skip AND revert any already-active setup), mirroring the `--stealth-vm-tuning`/`--no-stealth-vm-tuning` pattern via a new `LIVE_ATTACH_OVERRIDE` var; (c) gates the RX 9070 recommendation prompt on NO override given, so `--live-attach`/`--no-live-attach` short-circuit it cleanly. The revert runs regardless of GPU family, so the documented "Reversible with `--install-dynamic-binding`" now works for every card, not just RX 9070 / RDNA4.
  - `remove_live_attach` is now best-effort: it always `return 0`s (was returning 1 when nothing was removed, which would trip `set -e` at the bare call sites — the new dynamic opt-out, plus the existing `--install-early-binding` and `--reset` cleanup — when live-attach was never active).

The boot script is STANDALONE (never sources vfio.sh — at boot `/usr/local/bin/vfio` may be absent and sourcing would re-parse args). Installed + enabled + started-once-now automatically by `install_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning` whenever there is perf-hugepages ownership (this run reserved OR a prior run's registry still lists owned files — covers upgrading an existing hugepages setup that predates the service).

- 2026-08-25 20:00 UTC: Fixed a design flaw in the guest-GPU VM detection used by the stealth/perf tuners in `vfio.sh` — a live-attach VM (the recommended RDNA4 path) could not be tuned without reverting live-attach first:
  - root cause: `install_stealth_vm_tuning()`, `reset_stealth_vm_tuning()`, `stealth_vm_tuning_status()`, `install_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning()`, `reset_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning()`, and `ultimate_perf_vm_tuning_status()` all detected a guest-GPU VM by grepping the GPU PCI hostdev BDF out of the VM's persistent XML. But live-attach strips the GPU hostdev from the persistent XML (so the VM boots without the GPU and hot-attaches it later), so the BDF grep found nothing and the tuner reported "No shut-off VMs with the guest GPU found" — even though the VM clearly has the GPU (it's just hot-attached at runtime). The perf/stealth knobs (disk I/O, iothreads, cputune/numatune, topology, SMBIOS, NIC, etc.) have nothing to do with the GPU hostdev, so forcing a live-attach revert just to tune was the wrong UX. `install_virtio_win_guest_agent()` already had the correct fallback (BDF match OR in the live-attach VM list) with a comment explaining exactly this — the tuners just never got it.
  - fix: extracted the proven virtio-win detection pattern into a shared main-scope helper `_vm_is_guest_gpu_vm()` (inserted before `_vm_memory_kib()`): a VM qualifies if its XML has the GPU PCI hostdev (BDF match) OR it is in `/var/lib/vfio-dynamic/live-attach-vms`. Accepts an optional pre-fetched dumpxml as `$2` (avoids a redundant `virsh dumpxml` when the caller already has the XML). `VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_VM_LIST` env var overrides the list path for regression testing. All 6 tuner/status functions (stealth install/revert/status + ultimate-perf install/revert/status) now route through this helper instead of their own inline BDF-only awk parser. The R35 "nothing to tune" message now also names the live-attach VM list (mirroring the virtio-win installer message).
  - validation: `bash -n` clean; `shellcheck -S warning` clean (the now-unused `_bdfs` locals are SC2034 style-level, below warning). Extended `regression/ultimate-perf-regression.sh` with a Live-attach-aware detection section: static asserts (helper exists, honors `VFIO_LIVE_ATTACH_VM_LIST`, falls back to the list, R35 install uses the helper, "nothing to tune" message mentions the list) + 3 functional tests with a self-contained temp `CONF_FILE` + temp live-attach list (BDF match -> 0, no-BDF + in-list -> 0, no-BDF + not-in-list -> 1), no virsh/root needed (helper takes the XML as `$2`). rc=0. - 2026-08-25 19:40 UTC: Hardened the ultimate-performance hugepages reservation in `vfio.sh` (R35 follow-up: reserve-first + verify, RAM-change aware, reboot-safe re-reserve, 1GB guard, KiB-units fix):
  - **units fix (critical)**: the `<memoryBacking><hugepages><page>` element now emits `unit="KiB"` (was `unit="MiB"`) to match libvirt's XML convention (2048 KiB = 2 MiB = the standard 2MB hugepage). The old `MiB` made libvirt request 2048 MiB pages, which the kernel's `nr_hugepages` 2MB pool never satisfies. Conf key `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES_SIZE` is now documented as KiB (default `2048`); the helper param `_size_mib`→`_size_kib` (`_page_kib="$_size_kib"`); display lines divide by 1024 for MiB; README corrected from "default 2048 MiB" to "default 2048 KiB = 2 MiB".
  - **reserve-first + verify**: the host `nr_hugepages` reservation now runs BEFORE the python patcher (was after), and the helper re-reads `/proc/sys/vm/nr_hugepages` after the write to confirm the kernel delivered the full count. On a shortfall (fragmented RAM) it reverts the pool and returns 1 → the caller sets `_hp_vm=0` → the python skips `<memoryBacking>` so the VM keeps all other perf knobs and still starts, with a WARN naming the shortfall + the `hugepages=N` kernel-cmdline fallback (reboot-safe, can't be fragmented out).
  - **RAM-change aware**: the page count is recomputed from the VM's CURRENT `<memory>` each run via new `_vm_memory_kib()` (grow = add, shrink = free), instead of only ever adding. The count THIS VM owns is persisted to `${vm}_perf_hugepages_owned.txt`; target = sum of OTHER tuned VMs' owned + this VM's new need, so a RAM shrink frees exactly this VM's surplus while preserving other tuned VMs' reservations.
  - **reboot-safe re-reserve**: on a re-run where the VM already has `<hugepages>` but the host pool is 0 (post-reboot), the helper re-reserves instead of skipping, so `nr_hugepages=0` after a reboot no longer silently breaks `virsh start`.
  - **1GB guard**: `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES_SIZE >= 1048576` (KiB) → warns that 1GB pages need `hugepagesz=1G hugepages=N` on the kernel cmdline and skips the runtime write (the `nr_hugepages` knob only controls the 2MB pool), so no mismatched `<memoryBacking>` is added.
  - **rollback on skip paths**: on already-tuned/validate-fail/user-decline/define-fail, `_restore_host_hugepages_for_vm` removes the ownership file + recomputes the pool (except the idempotent already-tuned path which keeps ownership, since the VM still has hugepages).
  - validation: `bash -n` clean; embedded python `py_compile` clean; the patcher produces `virt-xml-validate`-passing XML on a mock 8GiB VM with `<page size="2048" unit="KiB">`, 4096-page math (8388608/2048), all stealth markers surviving, idempotent (exit 3 on re-run). Updated `regression/ultimate-perf-regression.sh` (fixed the stale `unit="MiB"` assertion → `unit="KiB"`; added a Hugepages-hardening section: KiB units + no-MiB-leak, `_vm_memory_kib` reads 8GiB→8388608 KiB + zero→0, RAM-change math 8GiB→4096 + 4GiB→2048, 1GB guard threshold+warn, reserve-first+verify documented + re-read + shortfall check, reboot-safe re-reserve, owned-file accounting, rollback-on-skip; FAIL SUMMARY preserved, rc=0) and `regression/ultimate-perf-smoke.sh` (added a hugepages-ON run asserting KiB units + stealth survives + validates, rc=0). README hugepages paragraph expanded additively (KiB units, reserve-first+verify, RAM-change aware, reboot-safe, 1GB guard) + the `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES_SIZE` default corrected from MiB to KiB.
- 2026-08-25 18:40 UTC: Added the ultimate-performance VM tuning installer (stealth-safe) to `vfio.sh` — a SEPARATE, more aggressive perf layer than the basic perf subset bundled inside `install_stealth_vm_tuning()`, applied to each shut-off guest-GPU VM while NEVER touching the stealth elements:
  - new `install_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning()` + `reset_ultimate_perf_vm_tuning()` + `ultimate_perf_vm_tuning_status()` (inserted after `stealth_vm_tuning_status()`), with two hugepages helpers `_reserve_host_hugepages_for_vm()` / `_restore_host_hugepages_for_vm()`. The perf python patcher (idempotent, exit 3 = no changes) touches only perf elements: disk `cache=none`+`io=native`+`discard=unmap` + virtio-blk multiqueue `queues=<vcpu>` + per-disk `iothread` round-robin; `<iothreads>` scaled to `min(vcpu,4)`; aggressive `cputune` (vcpupin/emulatorpin/iothreadpin from host topology) + `numatune` (memory `nodeset` strict-pinning ONLY when NUMA-safe — single node or all pinned vCPUs fit one node — otherwise skipped so the VM still starts; falls back to simple v->v pinning if host topology unreadable); CPU `<topology>` matching vCPU count + `<cache mode='passthrough'/>`; `currentMemory=memory` (no startup balloon, synergizes with stealth's `memballoon=none`); `<pm>` S3/S4 disabled. Hugepages is the ONLY host-RAM knob, OPT-IN (prompted default N, or `--ultimate-perf-hugepages`/`--no-ultimate-perf-hugepages`): adds `<memoryBacking><hugepages>` + reserves host `nr_hugepages` (prior value backed up to `${vm}_perf_hugepages_prev_<ts>.txt`, reverted by `--reset-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning`). AMD-gated; only shut-off VMs; `virt-xml-validate` + `prompt_yn` per VM before `virsh define`; `--dry-run` shows a `diff -u`.
  - **stealth coexistence guarantee**: the perf patcher never reads/writes `vendor_id`, `kvm/hidden`, `vmport`, `os/smbios`, `sysinfo`, `interface/model` (e1000e), `disk/serial`, `memballoon`, `clock/timer` (hypervclock/tsc), or `qemu:commandline` (-cpu/-smbios); it registers the `qemu`+`libosinfo` namespaces so ElementTree does not rewrite them to `ns0`. A stealth-tuned VM stays stealth-tuned through a perf pass; reverting perf restores a pre-perf backup that still contains stealth.
  - full CLI wiring mirroring the stealth pattern: new flags `--install-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning`, `--reset-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning`, `--ultimate-perf-hugepages`, `--no-ultimate-perf-hugepages`; MODE comment, `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES_OVERRIDE` var, fish/bash/zsh completions, usage one-liner + help blocks, parse_args, main dispatch, 2 new `--menu` entries (Apply/Revert ultimate-perf, renumbered Exit 12→14), `--detect`/`--verify` status block; 3 conf keys (`ULTIMATE_PERF_VM_BACKUP_DIR`, `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES`, `ULTIMATE_PERF_HUGEPAGES_SIZE`) with WHY notes.
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; embedded python `py_compile` clean; the patcher produces `virt-xml-validate`-passing XML on a mock VM (with stealth markers present), preserves all stealth markers (vendor_id, -cpu arg, disk serial, e1000e, memballoon=none, vmport off, hypervclock/TSC, qemu:commandline prefix), is idempotent (exit 3 on re-run), and hugepages opt-in adds `<memoryBacking>` + still validates. New `regression/ultimate-perf-regression.sh` (static wiring + functional python assertions + scoped numatune `nodeset`-not-`cpuset` check + FAIL SUMMARY, rc=0) and `regression/ultimate-perf-smoke.sh` (extract patcher + mock XML + virt-xml-validate + stealth survives + idempotency, rc=0); updated the R27 MODE-comment contiguous-substring assertion in `regression/live-attach-regression.sh` for the new `install-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning | reset-ultimate-perf-vm-tuning |` tokens. - 2026-08-12 21:10 UTC: Made the vBIOS ROM auto-injection section always print a prominent header + the guest GPU's PCI ID + the techpowerup Device Id serial + a deep link, and made every skip path a clear WARN, in `vfio.sh` (`install_vbios_romfile()`):
  - previously the `==== vBIOS ROM auto-injection ====` section header + PCI ID + techpowerup URL only printed on the SUCCESS path (a matching `.rom` was found); the skip paths (no `VBIOS/` folder, no `*.rom` files, no candidate match) only emitted quiet dim `note` lines with no header, so a user who had not yet dropped a `.rom` in the folder would see a few easy-to-miss dim lines and no section banner — and the success path's match description said "verify by eye" but never printed the URL to actually do so,
  - now the section header + `Guest GPU <BDF> PCI ID: <vendor:device>` + `techpowerup Device Id for vBIOS lookup: <vendor-device-subvendor-subdevice>` + `Find/verify a vBIOS dump at: <url>` print ONCE, up front, right after the guest GPU's PCI IDs are read from sysfs, so EVERY path below (no folder / no `.rom` files / no match / successful match) leaves the operator with the concrete serial + URL needed to find or verify a dump — the techpowerup Device Id serial (e.g. `1002-7550-1043-0614`) is now printed as a standalone copy-pasteable string alongside the deep link so it can be pasted directly into techpowerup's vgabios search box,
  - the three skip paths are now `WARN:`-prefixed (e.g. `WARN: no *.rom files found in <dir>; vBIOS ROM auto-injection skipped (no dump to pin).`) instead of quiet `note` lines, so a missing vBIOS dump is obvious in the install log instead of being a few dim lines that scroll by — the no-match path also repeats the techpowerup Device Id + URL so the operator can go find the right dump without scrolling back up,
  - the success path now also benefits from the up-front URL (the "verify by eye" match description is now actionable: the operator can click the already-printed techpowerup deep link to verify the best-effort family match is the right board/SKU),
  - the subsystem vendor/device IDs are read from sysfs (`/sys/bus/pci/devices/<BDF>/subsystem_vendor` + `subsystem_device`) and combined with the config-space vendor/device into the 4-part techpowerup Device Id; if the subsystem IDs are unreadable it falls back to the 2-part `vendor-device` form,
  - updated the `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` Q3z static assertion to verify the new up-front strings (`Find/verify a vBIOS dump at: $_tpu_url`, `techpowerup Device Id for vBIOS lookup: $_tpu_did`) AND the new `WARN: no *.rom files found` / `WARN: no VBIOS/ folder` skip strings are present (the old assertion checked for the now-removed `Find one at: $_tpu_url` line); the live `_vbios_techpowerup_url` deep-link test (simulated sysfs -> `?did=1002-7550-1043-0614`) is unchanged and still passes; `bash -n` + `shellcheck -S warning` clean in the edited region; `dynamic-binding-regression.sh` = "Dynamic binding regression checks passed." (no vBIOS string assertions there, so unaffected).
- 2026-08-12 21:00 UTC: Fixed a `set -euo pipefail` abort that skipped the stealth/perf VM tuning step during `--install-dynamic-binding --stealth-vm-tuning` in `vfio.sh`:
  - root cause: `install_vbios_romfile()` runs a `python3` heredoc that calls `sys.exit(3)` to signal "no changes needed" when a shut-off VM already has the vBIOS `<rom file=.../>` wired in; because `set -e` is active and the `python3` call was NOT in a conditional context, bash aborted the whole script with exit 3 immediately — before `local _py_status=$?` could run — so the script died right after "Installed vBIOS dump to ..." and never reached the stealth tuning step (step 8) that follows `install_vbios_romfile()` in `install_dynamic_binding_from_existing_config()`,
  - the same latent `set -e` hazard existed in `install_stealth_vm_tuning()` (python exit 3 = "already tuned") and `remove_vbios_romfile()` (python exit 3 = "nothing to remove"), so once the vBIOS step was unblocked, a VM that was already tuned would have killed the script the same way in the stealth step,
  - fix: capture the python3 exit status via `|| _py_status=$?` in all three heredocs (declared `local _py_status=0` before the call, appended `|| _py_status=$?` to the heredoc invocation, removed the now-redundant `local _py_status=$?` after it) so the non-zero exit is "handled" and `set -e` does not fire, while the existing `if (( _py_status == 3 ))` / `== 4` / `!= 0` branches keep their original semantics (3 = no changes needed, 4 = unsupported/not found, other non-zero = real patching failure),
  - also fixed a stale smoke-test assertion in `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` (Q3u case 5) that expected the old `install_hypervisor_hiding()` call to be wired into `install_dynamic_binding_from_existing_config`, but that call was intentionally replaced by `install_stealth_vm_tuning()` (which supersedes it with a realistic OEM `vendor_id=GENUINE00000` instead of `random`, avoiding a duplicate vendor_id) — the assertion now verifies the actual architecture: `install_stealth_vm_tuning` is defined + wired into the dynamic installer, uses `GENUINE00000`, and the old `install_hypervisor_hiding` call is NOT present in the dynamic path; the persistent `dynamic-binding-regression.sh` already had the correct Q3v assertions so it was unaffected,
  - validation: `bash -n` clean on `vfio.sh` and `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh`; `shellcheck -S warning` clean in the three edited heredoc regions (no new SC2181/SC2086); `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` = SMOKE SUMMARY: PASS (all cases including Q3u now pass); `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh` = "Dynamic binding regression checks passed." (all Q3u/Q3v/stealth assertions PASS, including `Q3v stealth tuning handles python exit 3 (no changes)` which confirms the `_py_status == 3` branch is preserved).
- 2026-08-10 18:37 UTC: Added vmport state=off + full-wizard stealth prompt to `vfio.sh`:
  - the stealth python tuning now sets `<vmport state='off'/>` in the `<features>` block — this disables the VMware backdoor port (I/O port 0x5658 'VMX') that Windows detection tools probe to report "Is Virtual Machine: yes"; combined with the existing CPUID hypervisor-bit disable + hyperv vendor_id spoofing, this closes the last major hypervisor detection vector in the VM XML (functional smoke verified: input `state='on'` → output `state='off'`),

  - `bash -n` + `shellcheck` + `python py_compile` clean; full `regression/script.sh` suite = 17 PASS / 0 FAIL with 5 new Q3v+ assertions (vmport.set state off, full wizard calls install_reboot_flr_monitor, full wizard calls install_stealth_vm_tuning, full wizard prompts for stealth tuning, prompt mentions vmport off).
- 2026-08-10 14:10 UTC: Added 7 improvements to the stealth/perf VM tuning in `vfio.sh` (building on the 13:55 absorption of the MIT-licensed Stealthy-VM tuning by Fredrik Bäckström):

  - #5 `--dry-run` diff: `install_stealth_vm_tuning()` now shows a `diff -u` of the current vs tuned XML per VM (before the redefine prompt) when `--dry-run` is set, so `--install-stealth-vm-tuning --dry-run` previews the exact XML changes,
  - #6 configurable `STEALTH_VM_BACKUP_DIR`: new conf key (default empty = `$HOME/Desktop`) persisted by `write_conf()`; `install_stealth_vm_tuning()` / `reset_stealth_vm_tuning()` read it from conf, fall back to `$HOME/Desktop`, then to `/var/lib/vfio-stealth-vm/backups` if the chosen dir is not writable; logs which backup dir is in use,
  - #7 README subsection: added a `### Stealth/perf VM tuning (SMBIOS / CPU / NIC / disk serials / iothreads)` subsection under "What's new" with a Quick links entry, documenting what it does (hypervisor hide, CPU, DMI-driven SMBIOS, devices, perf, preserves commandline), the verify-before-define safety flow, the `--stealth-vm-tuning`/`--no-stealth-vm-tuning`/`--install-stealth-vm-tuning`/`--reset-stealth-vm-tuning` flags, the `STEALTH_VM_BACKUP_DIR` key, and the MIT license attribution; additive, existing text intact,
  - validation: `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean on `vfio.sh`; embedded python compiles (`py_compile`); functional smoke verified #1 (Gigabyte/X670E/F32 SMBIOS from env vars) + #2 (kvmfr0 arg preserved through tune + re-tune) + idempotency (no duplicated `-cpu`/`-smbios`); full `regression/script.sh` suite = 17 PASS / 0 FAIL with ~30 new Q3v+ assertions covering all 7 improvements (DMI reads, preserve commandline, reset mode CLI/usage/completions/dispatch, detect/verify status, dry-run diff, backup dir, README); also fixed a parse_args case-label mangling (`--stealth-vm-tuning)` label had been wrongly set to `--install-usb-bt-mitigation)`).
- 2026-08-10 13:55 UTC: Absorbed the Stealthy-VM stealth/perf VM XML tuning into `vfio.sh` (option 3 — the tuning is now available directly from vfio.sh, applied to detected guest-GPU VMs at install time with verification before proceeding). The Stealthy-VM tuning is MIT-licensed (Copyright (c) 2026 Fredrik Bäckström); the algorithm is ported faithfully with attribution:
  - new `install_stealth_vm_tuning()` function that ports the Stealthy-VM `setup_vm_stealth.sh` python3 ElementTree tuning (profile=all: stealth + perf) into a heredoc'd invocation: hyperv `vendor_id=GENUINE00000` + `kvm hidden`, CPU `host-passthrough` + `hypervisor` CPUID-bit disable, SMBIOS type 0 (BIOS) + type 1 (system) spoofing with randomized serial/UUID, virtio→e1000e NIC, randomized disk serials, `memballoon=none`, `hypervclock` off, TSC native, QEMU `-cpu`/`-smbios` commandline args, plus perf (iothreads=1, host-aware cputune, disk iothread assignment); idempotent (rebuilds `<qemu:commandline>` from scratch),
  - detects guest-GPU VMs the same way `install_hypervisor_hiding()` does (virsh list --all --name, parse PCI hostdev BDFs, match GUEST_GPU_BDF from conf); only acts on shut-off VMs (running VMs are skipped with a note); AMD-gated (consistent with the existing hypervisor hide),
  - **verification before proceeding**: for each guest-GPU VM, dumps + backs up the XML, runs the python tuning on a temp copy, validates the tuned XML with `virt-xml-validate`, and `prompt_yn` (default N) before `virsh define`; if validation fails or the user declines, that VM is skipped (non-fatal); python exit 3 (no changes needed) is handled as "already tuned" success; python3 availability is checked,
  - supersedes `install_hypervisor_hiding()` in the dynamic install path (`install_dynamic_binding_from_existing_config`): the old `install_hypervisor_hiding()` call is replaced by an opt-in block gated on `STEALTH_VM_TUNING_OVERRIDE` (1=force on, 0=force skip, empty=prompt default N); `install_hypervisor_hiding()` is kept as a standalone fallback helper but no longer called from the dynamic path to avoid a duplicate `vendor_id` conflict (the stealth tuning uses `GENUINE00000` vs the old `random`),
  - new CLI flags: `--stealth-vm-tuning` (force on, skip prompt), `--no-stealth-vm-tuning` (force skip), and a standalone `--install-stealth-vm-tuning` mode (re-apply/refresh tuning on existing VMs without re-running the full wizard, like `--install-graphics-daemon`); all three are in `parse_args`, usage/help, and fish/bash/zsh completions,
  - fixed an idempotency bug in the ported python: removed the redundant `root.set('xmlns:qemu', QEMU_NS)` that caused a duplicate-attribute `ParseError` on re-parse (the `ET.register_namespace` alone is sufficient for the serializer to emit `xmlns:qemu`); re-running on an already-tuned VM no longer crashes (structural features are stable; only the randomized SMBIOS serial/UUID regenerate, matching the original Stealthy-VM behavior),
  - functional smoke verified: a mock VM XML through the python tuning adds all 8 features (vendor_id=GENUINE00000, kvm hidden, hypervisor CPUID disable, -cpu kvm=off, -smbios manufacturer=ASUS, e1000e NIC, Samsung_ disk serial, memballoon=none) and removes the tablet input; `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean on `vfio.sh`; embedded python compiles (`py_compile`); full `regression/script.sh` suite = 17 PASS / 0 FAIL,
  - added Q3v regression block (~30 assertions) to `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh`: function defined, CLI flags in parse_args/usage/completions (fish/bash/zsh), dynamic install calls `install_stealth_vm_tuning` (not `install_hypervisor_hiding`), verify-before-define (`virt-xml-validate` + `prompt_yn`), skips running VMs, python exit-3 handled, MIT license attribution, standalone MODE dispatch; updated the stale Q3u assertions (bash completion opts string, dynamic note step 8) for the new stealth tuning.

  - the optional prompts (USB/xHCI, boot framebuffer, the AMD 4-param power-tradeoff prompt, SELinux/AppArmor, ACS override, boot verbosity, boot target, custom kernel params) are NOT early-binding-specific — they apply to both modes — so they STILL RUN in dynamic mode (the earlier 13:34 version wrongly skipped them); a dynamic user still gets asked about ACS / framebuffer / SELinux / etc.,

  - verified the openSUSE dynamic path auto-lands exactly `amd_iommu=on iommu=pt vfio-pci.disable_idle_d3=1 pcie_port_pm=off pcie_aspm=off` (the user's current cmdline stability set) without a prompt, then still offers the optional both-mode prompts; `bash -n` + `shellcheck` clean; full `regression/script.sh` suite = 17 PASS / 0 FAIL.
- 2026-08-09 21:49 UTC: README — tightened the "Keeping the RX 9070 alive" subsection to lead with the practical result, additively (existing text kept intact): added a punchy blockquote "practical result" headline as the first thing in the subsection stating the two concrete outcomes (Windows soft reboot works; hard-killing the VM via `virsh destroy` / force-poweroff / BSOD works without the `Unknown PCI header type 127` zombie state) and naming the specific technique that makes the soft reboot reliable (pre-FLR Gen1 PCIe downtrain + adaptive post-FLR link restore, and why — without it the on-card switch fails to retrain the link after an FLR and the card wedges), plus two "Why soft reboot now works" / "Why hard kill now works" paragraphs tying each outcome to its mechanism (reboot-FLR monitor + Gen1-downtrain/adaptive restore for soft reboot; parked-on-vfio-pci + release-time remove+rescan + cooldown probe + alive-check for hard kill), and led the "What's new" intro with the same practical headline.
- 2026-08-09 21:40 UTC: README — surfaced the standout RX 9070 / RDNA4 work that was previously only in the changelog, additively (existing text kept intact):
  - added a prominent new `### Keeping the RX 9070 alive: soft reboot, hard kill, and the zombie card` subsection at the top of the "What's new" section and a matching Quick links entry, plus a one-sentence teaser in the "What's new" intro pointing to it,
  - the subsection strongly highlights the three concrete working mitigations that make the script more than a general VFIO helper: (1) the reboot-FLR monitor that keeps the RX 9070 alive through a guest soft reboot by doing a soft FLR to clear the display wedge without dropping the PCIe link, (2) the Gen1-downtrain + adaptive PCIe link restore technique (pre-FLR force Gen1, post-FLR restore to the hardware's actual max with bounded descent Gen5→Gen1, width logging, GPU alive-check, SKU resolution, VFIO_REBOOT_FLR_MAX_GEN cap), and (3) the three-layer zombie-card recovery for rapid stop/start (release-time remove+rescan, cooldown readiness probe, alive-check + clean abort instead of qemu's `Unknown PCI header type 127` crash),
  - added two Mermaid diagrams: a sequence diagram of the warm-reboot FLR + Gen1-downtrain + adaptive restore flow, and a color-styled flowchart of the stop/start zombie-recovery decision (green = ok, red = abort cleanly, blue = check), matching the existing README diagram style.
- 2026-08-09 21:22 UTC: Hardened the reboot-FLR monitor's RX 9070 family Gen1-downtrain / adaptive link restore in `vfio.sh` with 7 additive improvements (all gated to the RX 9070 family, vendor 1002 / device 7550):
  - SKU resolution: added `_rx9070_sku_name()` which reads the PCI revision from sysfs and maps the RX 9070 / 9070 XT / 9070 GRE (all share 0x7550) to a SKU name for diagnostic logging (observed rev 0xc0 -> 9070 XT, 0xc2 -> 9070 GRE, 0xc3 -> 9070 base; unknown revisions fall back to a labeled "unknown SKU" string); the pre-FLR jlog now includes the SKU plus the detected cap/cur/width, e.g. `pre-FLR Gen1 downtrain on upstream … (RX 9070 XT; cap=Gen5 cur=Gen4 width=x16)`,
  - defensive setpci parsing: added `_setpci_word()` which trims whitespace, validates hex, and left-pads to 4 digits before the `LnkCtl2` nibble slice, so `${_ctl2:3:1}` / `${_ctl2:0:3}` no longer assume a clean 4-char hex string from setpci (the old slice could grab the wrong nibble if setpci emitted leading/trailing whitespace or a short value); all `88.w`/`68.w`/`6A.w` reads in the monitor now go through this helper,
  - bounded descent: replaced the one-step restore fallback (`for _try in 1 2`, which only dropped one gen) with a bounded C-style `for (( _g = _target; _g >= 1; _g-- ))` descent that accepts the highest gen the link actually negotiates at — so a Gen5 target that only retrains at Gen4 will now also try Gen3 if Gen4 also fails, down to Gen1 (guaranteed by the pre-FLR downtrain); bounded (<= 6 iterations) so it can never loop forever; each gen attempt polls `current_link_speed` for up to ~0.6s,
  - `VFIO_REBOOT_FLR_MAX_GEN` operator cap: new conf key (default empty = no cap, 1..6 clamps) in `/etc/vfio-gpu-passthrough.conf` via `write_conf()`, read by the restore to clamp the target for boards that reliably retrain at a lower gen but not the card's full capability (e.g. a Gen5 card in a board that only retrains Gen4 after an FLR) so it skips the failing higher-gen attempt every warm reboot,
  - link width logging: added `_port_link_width()` reading sysfs `current_link_width`; the negotiated/restore-OK/degraded diagnostics now include the width (e.g. `link negotiated at Gen4 x8`), so an x16->x8 degradation from a bad slot/cable is visible alongside the speed (the speed check alone cannot see a width problem),
  - GPU-endpoint alive-check: added `_gpu_alive()` (vendor sysfs not 0xffff + config space not all-ff) run after the restore to catch the case where the LINK is back at a good gen but the GPU itself is still wedged; logs a non-fatal `GPU endpoint not alive after FLR restore … a host reboot may be needed` WARN (does not fail the FLR since the display-wedge clear already succeeded),
  - stronger setpci-missing notice: `install_reboot_flr_monitor()` now checks for `setpci` (pciutils) at install time and prints a clear note naming the distro install command (openSUSE `zypper in pciutils`, Fedora `dnf install pciutils`, Debian `apt-get install pciutils`, Arch `pacman -S pciutils`) when it is absent, so the warm-reboot fix being silently inactive is obvious instead of only logged at FLR time,
  - `do_flr()` resets the new `_FLR_DETECTED_WIDTH` / `_FLR_SKU_NAME` stash at entry alongside the existing stash vars so a previous run can't leak in.
  - updated `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh` Q3t (61 assertions) and `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` Q3t (10 cases): added coverage for `_rx9070_sku_name` (rev c0/c2/c3/c9 -> XT/GRE/base/unknown via mock sysfs revision), `_setpci_word` defensive trim+pad (`  45 ` -> 0045, `5` -> 0005), `_port_link_width` (mock current_link_width=16), `VFIO_REBOOT_FLR_MAX_GEN` clamp (5->4 with cap, 5 with no cap), bounded descent multi-step drop (Gen5 target / Gen3 hw -> 3, which the old one-step fallback could not do), `_gpu_alive` wiring, width in diagnostics, the write_conf default, the installer setpci/pciutils notice, and that the old `_post_flr_restore_gen5` name, `${_saved_hi}5` value, and `for _try in 1 2` one-step loop are all gone.
- 2026-08-09 21:10 UTC: Made the reboot-FLR monitor's pre-FLR Gen1 downtrain / post-FLR link restore hardware-adaptive and documented the RX 9070 family coverage in `vfio.sh`:
  - the `_RX9070_DEVICE_ID` gate comment now documents that the RX 9070, RX 9070 XT, and RX 9070 GRE ALL share PCI device ID `0x7550` (Navi 48 / gfx1201 — same ASIC, differentiated by subsystem/BIOS/revision, not by base device ID), so the existing `7550` gate already covers the whole consumer 9070 family; `0x7551` (Radeon AI PRO R9700, same Navi 48 silicon) is noted as a non-consumer SKU the adaptive restore would handle identically if added,
  - added `_speed_to_gen()` + `_port_speed_gen()` helpers that parse the kernel sysfs `max_link_speed` / `current_link_speed` strings (e.g. "16 GT/s") into a PCIe generation integer (1=Gen1 .. 6=Gen6) so the restore reads the real hardware capability instead of guessing config-space offsets,
  - `_pre_flr_gen1_downtrain()` now detects the upstream switch port's link capability and current negotiated speed (stashed in `_FLR_DETECTED_CAP` / `_FLR_DETECTED_CUR`) and saves the original `LnkCtl2` target nibble (`_FLR_SAVED_TARGET`) / full `LnkCtl2` (`_FLR_SAVED_CTL2`) before forcing Gen1, so the restore can adapt to the actual hardware (Gen4 slot/mobo vs Gen5 card) instead of blindly forcing Gen5,
  - replaced the hardcoded Gen5-only `_post_flr_restore_gen5()` with `_post_flr_restore_target()`, which chooses the restore target in priority order: pre-FLR negotiated speed when below the port's capability (slower slot / signal-integrity / Gen4 board — keeps the proven gen) > detected capability > saved `LnkCtl2` target nibble > Gen5 last-resort default, clamped to a sane Gen1–Gen6 range,
  - the new restore verifies the negotiated speed after retrain and, if the link did NOT reach the target (a hardware/signal-integrity problem), drops one generation and retries once (bounded) so the policy ADAPTS instead of leaving the link degraded or failing the FLR; if it still settles below the detected capability it logs a `possible hardware/signal-integrity issue — link up but degraded` diagnostic for the operator without failing the FLR (the display-wedge clear already succeeded),
  - `do_flr()` resets the detection stash at entry (so a previous run can't leak in) and now calls the adaptive restore; comments updated from "Gen5"/"Gated to RX 9070 ONLY" to reflect the adaptive Gen4/Gen5 restore + RX 9070 family gating,
  - updated `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh` Q3t and `regression/dynamic-binding-smoke.sh` Q3t to assert the family comment, the `_speed_to_gen`/`_port_speed_gen` helpers, the `_FLR_DETECTED_CAP`/`_FLR_DETECTED_CUR`/`_FLR_SAVED_TARGET` detection, the adaptive restore target selection (Gen5 slot / Gen4 slot / degraded link / saved-only / none -> Gen5), the degraded-link fallback, the hardware-problem diagnostic, and that the old `_post_flr_restore_gen5` name and hardcoded `${_saved_hi}5` value are gone; smoke test exercises the speed-string -> gen parser and adaptive target picker with a standalone script.

  - new `--binding-mode early|dynamic` install-mode override flag with `normalize_binding_mode_arg()`, `parse_args` cases, usage/help, and fish/bash/zsh completions,

  - the hook reads the VM domain XML from stdin, extracts PCI `<hostdev>` addresses with an awk parser (`extract_hostdev_bdfs()`), and only switches the guest GPU to vfio-pci (`--bind-now`) when the configured guest GPU BDF is actually attached to that VM; unrelated VMs pass through untouched,
  - generated `vfio-bind-selected-gpu.sh` refactored with `--bind-now`/`--release` arg parsing and a dynamic-mode boot no-op guard so the existing systemd unit stays harmless in dynamic mode,
  - `bind_one()` now sets `d3cold_allowed=0` per-device (RX 9070 / RDNA4 reset-bug fix) and verifies the device actually landed on vfio-pci after bind, returning non-zero on failure so the libvirt hook aborts the VM start cleanly; `do_bind()` propagates the failure for both the guest GPU and audio functions,
  - dynamic mode skips/strips `vfio-pci.ids=` and `rd.driver.pre=vfio-pci` from the kernel cmdline in all 3 bootloader paths (openSUSE `/etc/kernel/cmdline`, systemd-boot entry, GRUB) while keeping IOMMU and AMD stability params,
  - new standalone CLI modes `--install-dynamic-binding` and `--install-early-binding` (mirror `--install-graphics-daemon`) to switch an existing setup between binding modes without re-running the full wizard: flip the conf key, install/remove the hook, strip/re-add early-binding cmdline tokens, sync BLS,
  - new `maybe_offer_detect_dynamic_binding()` detect-mode remediation that offers to switch to dynamic binding when an AMD guest GPU is still on amdgpu with reset-bug markers in kernel logs and the current mode is early,
  - `--verify` no longer fails the guest-GPU-not-on-vfio-pci check in dynamic mode (expected on amdgpu until VM start) and instead checks the hook script + qemu entry presence; `--detect` reports binding mode and libvirt hook status,
  - `--reset` and rollback now remove/restore the libvirt qemu hook (restoring any pre-existing user-managed hook from `.bak` backup),
  - wizard binding-mode prompt now shows a readable early-vs-dynamic comparison column (trade-offs) and defaults to dynamic when an AMD guest GPU is currently on amdgpu,
  - added `regression/dynamic-binding-regression.sh` with static wiring + functional assertions (VM-XML detection, dynamic cmdline stripping, unbind/bind flow, post-bind verification, failure propagation) ending with a FAIL SUMMARY block.
